# Changelog archive: v3.x

Releases older than the current major, moved verbatim out of [`CHANGELOG.md`](../../CHANGELOG.md). Current and unreleased entries stay in `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v3.16.0 - 2026-10-17


### Tracked issues

- [#541](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/541) — [Bug] genexus_create descarta description, firstItemType (SDT) e dataType/length (Attribute) e responde sucesso; atributo nasce NUMERIC(4) sem conserto pelo MCP
- [#542](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/542) — [Bug][Integridade] SDT: trocar o tipo de membro existente - update_visual falha sem dizer o campo; genexus_edit part=Structure grava pela metade (VARCHAR(40) -> VARCHAR(7)) e nao desfaz
- [#543](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/543) — [Bug] genexus_variable add typeName=<SDT> em objeto existente: VariableTypeNotPersisted, enquanto o patch da parte Variables grava a mesma linha
- [#544](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/544) — [Bug] WriteNotPersisted falso em Layout (WebForm e Report) que persistiu: id de folha descartado, &var -> var:N, ControlName segue o AttributeReference
- [#545](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/545) — [Bug/Feature] WorkWithPlus em Transaction: mover atributo do form e recusado como "identity changed" (contradiz o hint); remocao so uma por import; sem acao tipada
- [#546](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/546) — [Bug/DX] genexus_lifecycle rebuild aceita target e o dryRun mostra wouldBuild:[target] junto com action RebuildAll
- [#547](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/547) — [Bug][Follow-up #448] Patch de Variables sobrepõe semânticas de saved e orienta retry após Save confirmado
- [#548](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/548) — [Bug][Follow-up #479] Diagnóstico marca persisted=true ao enfileirar e aponta detalhes que não estão no log
- [#549](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/549) — [Bug][Follow-up #479] Fonte simples em exceção sintética atravessa a sanitização do diagnóstico de leitura
- [#550](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/550) — [Bug][Follow-up #479] SanitizeStackTrace pula o limite de caracteres quando também limita frames
- [#551](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/551) — WWP Transaction: cannot add textBlock (or grid row userAction / selection parameters) via import_part or genexus_wwp
- [#552](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/552) — WWP Transaction: cannot create tabs (add_tab -> TabsContainerNotFound) nor move attributes into a tab
- [#553](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/553) — [Bug][Follow-up #522] Uma falha de leitura por objeto trava o backfill do source store em error: tolerar como falha por objeto, não como fatia fatal
- [#554](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/554) — [Bug][Follow-up #522] Os contadores do backfill do source store somam acumuladores locais a cada iteração: processed/stored/stale crescem triangular e o removido ganha contagem extra
- [#555](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/555) — [DX][Minor][Follow-up #527] A mensagem AmbiguousName de genexus_impact/genexus_object_context não sugere type quando ele reduz a lista a um único candidato
- [#556](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/556) — Nexus IDE checks: aborta cedo com Error mutex already exists (user-data-dir de single-instance compartilhado) e trunca a suite no lote paralelo do release-preflight
- [#557](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/557) — [Bug][Follow-up #525] add_attribute sem descrição em mode=ops responde WriteNotPersisted quando verifyMode é omitido; com verifyMode:"semantic" a mesma gravação passa
- [#558](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/558) — [Bug][Follow-up #217] A reversão de uma gravação de Structure em mode=ops falha com SDK-API-UNSUPPORTED, embora genexus_structure remove_attribute remova o mesmo atributo
- [#559](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/559) — [Feature] genexus_workwith: autoria tipada de filtro de Selection (filterAttribute + conditions) no WorkWith padrão
- [#560](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/560) — [Feature] WWP Transaction: prompt de inserção do DVelop Combo (<prompt><parameters>) e reposicionar userAction/contêiner — hoje só pela IDE


- Experimental integral-second Rust DateTime path matches 42 native outputs; Date month/year shifts match another 18. Timezone/millisecond/general SDK compatibility remain open.


- Experimental Rust Date compiler/runtime: 37 source-matched native observations, explicit two-digit-year window, typed values, empty/components and checked day arithmetic. DateTime/general calendar and full SDK parity remain unimplemented.


### Added

- Standalone Rust `gx-engine` packaging: `build.ps1` builds and bundles `gx-engine.exe` into the publish artifact root (`publish\gx-engine.exe`) alongside `gxwire.exe`.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds Data Views external table mapping and multi-channel execution output buffering (Rows H4, G11):
  - Data Views & External Table Mapping (`src/gx-engine/src/dataview.rs`): models GeneXus Data Views mapping internal Knowledge Base attributes and tables to external database tables, views, and columns. Parses clean DataView DSL with `table`, `external_name`, `platform`, `datastore`, `fields { Att => col : Type; }`, and `index Name { external = ...; primary/unique = true; Att; }` with comment stripping (`//`, `/* */`) and JSON fallback. Provides case-insensitive bidirectional mapping (`map_attribute_to_column`, `map_column_to_attribute`), primary/unique index preservation, fail-closed validation, and dialect-accurate external SQL query rendering for ANSI, SQL Server, PostgreSQL, and Oracle with identifier quoting and optional `WHERE` clause. Admitted `ObjectKind::DataView` in the portable KB catalog and text-tree interop (`.dv` files). Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/data_view.rs`.
  - Output Channels & Streaming Buffers (`src/gx-engine/src/output_channel.rs`): provides structured multi-channel message routing across `Console`, `WebBuffer`, `Log`, and `StatusLine` output channels matching GeneXus execution environments. Supports `msg(&text, status)` with integer status codes, `latest_status()` inspection, newline-separated console flushes (`flush_console_to_string`), continuous web stream flushes (`flush_web_to_string`), channel filtering, and bounded streaming chunk partitioning (`render_streaming_chunks`) for real-time MCP streaming. Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/output_channel.rs`.
  - Promoted `dataview_external_table_mapping` and `output_channels_streaming_buffers` capabilities to Parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry`. Data Views (Row H4) and Output Channels (Row G11) reach Parity, advancing overall weighted SDK replacement from 69.6% to 73.2% (50/56 areas touched: 89.3%). Portable gate: 481 tests passed; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds structured diagnostics catalog & source location model and full text tree export/import engine (resolving Issue #499 for GxWire):
  - Structured Diagnostics Catalog & Location Model (`src/gx-engine/src/diagnostics_catalog.rs`): models official GeneXus diagnostic codes (`spc0004`, `spc0005`, `spc0006`, `spc0009`, `spc0010`, `spc0023`, `spc0034`, `spc0051`, `spc0216`, `src0295`) with source location tracking (`file`, `line`, `column`, `length`), severity levels (`Error`, `Warning`, `Information`, `Hint`), and categories (`Syntax`, `TypeCheck`, `Specification`, `Database`, `Reorganization`, `Naming`, `Security`). Implements `DiagnosticBag` with fail-closed error checking (`has_errors()`), severity filtering, category grouping, machine-readable JSON serialization/deserialization, and Clang/Rust-style terminal diagnostic rendering with source extraction and carets (`^`) pointing to exact column positions. Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/diagnostics_catalog.rs`.
  - Text Tree Export & Import Engine (`src/gx-engine/src/text_tree.rs`): bridges the static code indexing gap for GxWire (resolving Issue #499) by serializing/deserializing entire Knowledge Base catalogs to and from readable disk trees (`Objects/Procedures/<name>.prc`, `Objects/Transactions/<name>.trn`, `Definitions/SDTs/<name>.sdt`, `Definitions/Domains/<name>.dom`, `Definitions/Attributes/<name>.att`, `Objects/DataProviders/<name>.dp`, `Objects/DataSelectors/<name>.ds`, `Objects/DesignSystems/<name>.dso`). Supports full catalog round-trips (`Catalog` -> `TextTreeSnapshot` -> `Catalog`), filesystem recursive export/import with directory creation, and extension validation. Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/text_tree.rs`.
  - Promoted `structured_diagnostics_catalog` and `text_tree_roundtrip` capabilities to Parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry`. Diagnostics Model (Row C4) and Interoperability & Tooling (Row I3) reach Parity, advancing overall weighted SDK replacement from 66.1% to 69.6% (49/56 areas touched: 87.5%). Portable gate: 461 tests passed; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds extended primitive types (GUID 128-bit & Math functions) and Design System Object (DSO) tokens & styles engine matching official GeneXus runtime and DSO semantics:
  - Extended Primitive Types & Math Utilities (`src/gx-engine/src/extended_types.rs`): implements 128-bit RFC 4122 v4 `GuidValue` with case-insensitive parsing of 36-char hyphenated and 32-char unhyphenated hex formats, standard GUID operations (`Guid.NewGuid()`, deterministic `Guid.new_v4(seed)`, `Guid.FromString(s)`, `.IsEmpty()`, `.SetEmpty()`, and lowercase 36-char `.ToString()`). Provides mathematical utility functions: `math_mod` (division remainder matching GeneXus `Mod(n1, n2)` and `&n1.Mod(&n2)` with fail-safe zero divisor handling and `-0.0` normalization), `math_pow` (`&base.Pow(&exp)`), `math_sqrt` (`&n.Sqrt()`, failing closed on negative numbers), `math_abs` (`&n.Abs()`), and `math_random` (pseudo-random float strictly in `[0.0, 1.0)`). Simulates variable method dispatch (`&guid.IsEmpty()`, `&num.Sqrt()`). Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/extended_types.rs`.
  - Design System Object (DSO) Engine (`src/gx-engine/src/design_system.rs`): parses full modern DSO grammar with `@import` / `styles(ParentDSO)`, `tokens [TokenSet] { #group { name: value; } }`, and CSS style rules (`.Class { property: $token; }`). Implements clean comment stripping, scoped token resolution (`$colors.primary`, `$primary`, parent DSO inheritance), and clean CSS stylesheet rendering with fail-closed missing token validation. Admitted `ObjectKind::DesignSystem` in the portable KB catalog. Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/design_system.rs`.
  - Promoted `guid_and_extended_math` and `design_system_tokens_styles` capabilities to Parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry`. Extended Types (Row G9) and Design System Objects (Row B8, J1) reach Parity, advancing overall weighted SDK replacement from 62.5% to 66.1% (47/56 areas touched: 83.9%). Portable gate: 441 tests passed; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds DataProvider declarative specification and catalog-wide object cross-reference & impact analysis matching official GeneXus DataProvider and dependency analysis semantics:
  - DataProvider Declarative Engine (`src/gx-engine/src/dataprovider.rs`): parses full DataProvider grammar with `parm(...)` interface rules, bracketed properties (`[Count = 10]`, `[Paging = 20]`), nested group hierarchies (`{ ... }`), and item assignments (`Field = expr`). Cleanly strips comments (`//`, `/* */`) while preserving string literals containing semicolons, slashes, and escaped quotes. Evaluates DataProvider hierarchies against parameter values into clean JSON matching target SDT schemas. Performs compile-time schema validation against catalog SDT definitions, failing closed on unknown fields. Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/data_provider.rs`.
  - Object Cross-Reference & Impact Analysis Engine (`src/gx-engine/src/impact.rs`): indexes multi-kind relationships (`CallsProcedure`, `UsesSdt`, `ReferencesAttribute`, `ReadsTable`, `UpdatesTable`, `InsertsTable`, `DeletesTable`) across procedures, transactions, and tables. Calculates cycle-safe transitive callers and dependents up to depth 16 (`MAX_TRANSITIVE_DEPTH`). Pinpoints all objects affected by attribute and physical table schema alterations. Computes blast radius with direct/transitive caller counts, touched artifacts, and risk level classifications (`Low`, `Medium`, `High`, `Critical`). Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/impact_analysis.rs`.
  - Promoted `dataprovider_specification` and `object_impact_analysis` capabilities to Parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry`. DataProvider (Row B5) and Dependency Graph & Impact (Row D4) reach Parity, advancing overall weighted SDK replacement from 58.9% to 62.5% (45/56 areas touched: 80.4%). Portable gate: 421 tests passed; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds database reorganization schema diffing and hierarchical module qualified name resolution matching official GeneXus reorganizer and namespace semantics:
  - Database Reorganization & Schema Diffing (`src/gx-engine/src/reorg.rs`): multi-table schema diffing engine compares physical schema snapshots and identifies added, dropped, altered, or unchanged tables. Tracks column additions, removals, data type alterations, and nullability modifications. Manages secondary index and foreign key constraint lifecycles. Generates dialect-accurate DDL migration scripts (`ALTER TABLE ... ADD/DROP/MODIFY/ALTER COLUMN`, `CREATE/DROP TABLE`, `ADD/DROP CONSTRAINT`, `CREATE/DROP INDEX`) across ANSI, SQL Server, PostgreSQL, and Oracle dialects. Emits explicit risk warnings for destructive operations (dropped tables and dropped columns). Read-only migration plan generation with zero SQL execution against live databases. Verified by 9 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/reorg_impact.rs`.
  - Hierarchical Modules & Qualified Name Resolution (`src/gx-engine/src/module.rs`): implements `QualifiedName` parsing and validation (e.g. `Sales.Invoicing.Invoice`), hierarchical module tree DAG with parent-child links, cycle detection, and recursion depth limit of 16. Enforces GeneXus object resolution rules: local module precedence for bare identifiers, exact qualified name matching, global fallback for unique names, and fail-closed `spc0006` diagnostics (*"Ambiguous object reference '{query}'; qualified name required (candidates: ...)"*) when homonyms exist across distinct modules. Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/module_resolution.rs`.
  - Promoted `database_reorganization` and `module_namespaces` capabilities to Parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry`. KB Storage & Namespaces (Row A4) and Database Reorganization (Row D3, H1) reach Parity, advancing overall weighted SDK replacement from 55.4% to 58.9% (43/56 areas touched: 76.8%). Portable gate: 401 tests passed; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds Relational DML query planning and DataSelector query composition matching official GeneXus specifier and DML semantics:
  - Relational DML Planning (`src/gx-engine/src/dml.rs`): plans procedural updates (`Attribute = expr`), deletes (`delete`), and row insertions (`new ... [when duplicate ...] endnew`) with primary key validation, fail-closed diagnostics (`spc0034` rejecting primary key mutation in `For Each`, `spc0023` rejecting incomplete primary key specification on insert), and multi-dialect SQL generation for ANSI, SQL Server (`IF NOT EXISTS`), PostgreSQL (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), and Oracle (`MERGE`). Generates clean read-only DML plans with zero SQL writes against live databases. Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/dml_navigation.rs`.
  - DataSelector Composition (`src/gx-engine/src/dataselector.rs`): parses `DataSelector` objects covering `parm(...)` rules, `order ...` clauses, and `where ...` conditions. Supports `For each using DataSelectorName(&args...)` syntax in procedures, recursive AST parameter substitution, filter/order query composition, and base table re-inference. Verified by 9 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/data_selector.rs`.
  - Catalog & Navigation Integration: admitted `ObjectKind::DataSelector` in portable catalog, added `dml_plans` and `using_data_selector` to `NavigationLevel`, and `standalone_dml` to `NavigationReport`.
  - Promoted `dml_navigation` and `dataselector_composition` capabilities to Parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry`. Specifier & SQL Navigation subsystem reaches 100.0% parity (8/8 areas: C1, C2, D1, D2, D3, D4, H1, H2), and overall weighted SDK replacement moves from 51.8% to 55.4% (41/56 areas touched: 73.2%). Portable gate: 382 tests passed; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds formula specification and aggregate navigation query planning matching official GeneXus formula semantics: implements aggregate formulas (`Sum`, `Count`, `Max`, `Min`, `Find`, `Average`/`Avg`) with target attribute or horizontal expression, conditional filters, and default values. Implements automatic context correlation for formulas nested inside `For Each` queries via foreign keys and transitive extended table reachability, selecting optimal secondary or primary indexes for correlation columns. Implements standalone aggregate formulas outside `For Each` blocks, intra-row and cross-table horizontal formulas (`LinePrice * LineQty`), and Transaction attribute formulas (`TransactionAttributeRef.formula`). Emits official fail-closed specifier diagnostics: `spc0004` (No relationship between attributes) and `spc0005` (Ambiguous base table). Generates read-only multi-dialect SELECT queries with exact aggregate syntax (`SUM`, `COUNT`, `MAX`, `MIN`, `AVG`, `TOP 1`, `LIMIT 1`, `ROWNUM = 1`) for ANSI, SQL Server (`[...]`), PostgreSQL (`"..."`), and Oracle. Adheres strictly to zero SQL writes. Verified by 11 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/formula_navigation.rs`. Promoted `formula_navigation` capability to Parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry`. Specifier & SQL Navigation subsystem reaches 87.5% parity (7/8 areas: C1, C2, D1, D2, D3, D4, H2), and overall weighted SDK replacement moves from 50.0% to 51.8% (39/56 areas touched: 71.4%). Portable gate: 363 tests passed; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds Specifier base table inference, Navigation query plan engine, and `For Each` relational data access matching official GeneXus specifier and navigation semantics: implements full procedural `For Each` AST and parsing with explicit attributes, multi-clause `Where` filters, `Order` items with `DESC`/`NONE`, `Defined By` attribute, loop body, and `When None` blocks ending in `EndFor`. Adds scoped `allow_attributes` parser mode (attributes are admitted inside `For Each`, while bare identifiers outside continue to fail closed). Relational navigation engine (`navigation.rs`) calculates extended table (Tabela Estendida) foreign key reachability paths up to depth 16, infers minimal base tables (prioritizing direct-matching candidate tables and applying subordination pruning), selects optimal primary and secondary indexes, automatically resolves foreign key joins, correlates nested loops on matching keys, and generates read-only multi-dialect SELECT queries with canonical column casing for ANSI, SQL Server (`[...]`), PostgreSQL (`"..."`), and Oracle. Emits official fail-closed specifier diagnostics: `spc0004` (No relationship between attributes) and `spc0005` (Ambiguous base table; multiple tables qualify, use 'Defined by'). Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/navigation_specifier.rs` and 13 unit tests in `src/gx-engine/src/frontend.rs`. Promoted `sql_navigation` capability to Parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry`. Specifier & SQL Navigation subsystem reaches 75.0% parity (6/8 areas: C1, C2, D1, D2, D3, D4), and overall weighted SDK replacement moves from 47.0% to 50.0% (38/56 areas touched: 69.6%). Portable gate: 352 tests passed; Clippy and rustfmt clean. Zero SQL writes.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds physical table schema normalization and multi-dialect SQL DDL generation matching official GeneXus database reorganizer and schema generator semantics: normalizes Transaction levels into physical relational `Table`s with primary keys (`TableKey`), foreign keys (`TableForeignKey`), non-stored formula attribute exclusion (virtual calculated fields are excluded from physical columns), and non-unique secondary indexes for foreign keys. Supports 4 SQL dialects (`Ansi`, `SqlServer`, `PostgreSql`, `Oracle`) with exact identifier quoting (`[Identifier]`, `"Identifier"`, unquoted uppercase for Oracle), type mappings (`INT`/`BIGINT`/`NVARCHAR`/`BIT`/`DATETIME2` for SQL Server, `INTEGER`/`BIGINT`/`VARCHAR`/`BOOLEAN`/`TIMESTAMP` for PostgreSQL, `NUMBER(p,s)`/`VARCHAR2`/`NUMBER(1)`/`TIMESTAMP` for Oracle), primary key constraint generation, and foreign key constraints with `ON DELETE NO ACTION` / `CASCADE`. Supports catalog-wide table derivation (`derive_tables_from_catalog`) resolving cross-transaction foreign keys by singular primary key matching (e.g. `Invoice.CustomerId` referencing `Customer.CustomerId`), chained `BasedOn` domain/attribute types, and SDK `ExportFile` XML exchange. Verified by 9 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/table_ddl.rs` (dialect type mappings, validation rules, single-level lowering, multi-level hierarchy with inherited keys/FKs, 3-level depth, catalog attribute resolution, cross-transaction foreign key inference, full database DDL generation, and `ExportFile` XML exchange). New `physical_table_ddl` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer). Specifier & SQL Navigation subsystem reaches 25.0% parity (2/8 areas: Table Schema Normalization, Multi-Dialect SQL DDL), and overall weighted SDK replacement moves from 45.2% to 47.0% (36/56 areas touched: 64.3%). Portable gate: 342 tests passed; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds database transaction control procedural statements (`Commit` and `Rollback`) matching official GeneXus procedure semantics: parses both bare statements (`Commit`, `Rollback`) and call syntax (`Commit()`, `Rollback()`) with optional semicolons across top-level procedure blocks, conditionals (`If ... Else ... EndIf`), loops (`For`, `For In`, `Do While`), and local subroutines (`Sub ... EndSub`). Emits transactional runtime lowering hooks (`// commit;` and `// rollback;`) preserving control flow and procedure return analysis. Fails closed on format_version 1 programs. Integrates with the portable KB catalog and SDK `ExportFile` XML exchange. Verified by 7 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/transaction_control.rs` (statement variants, conditionals, loops, subroutines, while loops, format_version 1 fail-closed rejection, compiled executable execution, and ExportFile XML exchange). New `transaction_control` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer). Overall weighted SDK replacement moves from 44.6% to 45.2% (34/56 areas touched: 60.7%). Portable gate: 333 tests passed; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds GeneXus Rules part (`9b0a32a3-de6d-4be1-a4dd-1b85d3741534`) parser, AST, and Parm interface parity across the portable KB catalog and SDK `ExportFile` XML: parses full Rules grammar for Procedures and Transactions including `Parm(in|out|inout:&Var, ...)`, `Error('msg' [, cond])`, `Msg('msg' [, cond])`, `Default(att, val)`, `NoAccept(att)`, `Accept(att)`, `Order(att1, ... | none)`, `Prompt('PromptObject', &out1, ...)`, `Serial(att, step[, range])`, and `Hidden(att)`. Cleanly strips single-line (`//`) and multi-line (`/* */`) comments, preserves string literals containing semicolons and escaped quotes (`''`, `\"`), and enforces bounded ASCII identifiers. Enforces strict scope validation: Procedures require Parm arguments to be `&variables` (attributes in procedure Parm are rejected) and prohibit Transaction-only rules (`Default`, `Serial`, `Hidden`, `NoAccept`). Fails closed on invalid syntax: rejects `Unique` with `src0295` and index guidance, rejects duplicate `Parm` rules, rejects unclosed comments/strings, and rejects missing semicolons. Integrates seamlessly into the portable KB catalog and SDK `ExportFile` XML exchange. Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/rules_parity.rs`. New `rules_and_parm_parity` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer). Portable KB Catalog & XML Projection subsystem reaches 100.0% parity (6/6 areas: Procedures, SDTs, Domains, Attributes, Transactions, Rules), and overall weighted SDK replacement moves from 42.5% to 44.6% (33/56 areas touched: 58.9%). Portable gate: 326 tests passed; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds Transaction (`1db606f2-af09-4cf9-a3b5-b481519d28f6`) structure, primary keys, and multi-level hierarchy projection in portable catalog and SDK `ExportFile` XML: parses and validates `TransactionStructure` and nested `TransactionLevel`s, enforcing primary key constraints on root and child levels (`IsKey = true`), bounded recursion depth of 16, unique level and attribute names, and identifier hygiene. Lowers Transaction levels into Business Component `RecordType` schemas for direct procedure variable access and typed manipulation (`&Customer.CustomerId = 42`). Resolves attribute data types against catalog `Attribute` and `Domain` objects when not declared inline. SDK `ExportFile` XML automatically parses `<TransactionStructure>`, extracts level attributes, formulas, nullability, and foreign key flags, ignores standard metadata parts (Layout, Events, Help), and connects explicit `Uses` references. Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/transaction_structure.rs` (nested levels, root and child primary key enforcement, attribute references, recursion depth limit 16, Business Component `RecordType` schema lowering, procedure interoperability). New `transaction_structure_projection` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer). Portable KB Catalog & XML Projection subsystem moves from 66.7% to 83.3% (5/6 areas: Procedures, SDTs, Domains, Attributes, Transactions), and overall weighted SDK replacement moves from 40.7% to 42.5% (33/56 areas touched: 58.9%). Portable gate: 316 tests passed; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds Domain (`00972a17-9975-449e-aab1-d26165d51393`) and Attribute (`adbb33c9-0906-4971-833c-998de27e0676`) projection, dependency resolution, and `BasedOn` typing across the portable KB catalog and SDK `ExportFile` XML: resolves `BasedOn` domain chains (up to bounded depth 16) and attribute references into concrete `DataType`s for procedure variables, parameter signatures, return types, and SDT fields at compilation boundaries while preserving zero-overhead lowering. Enforces strict GeneXus semantic rules: domains cannot be based on attributes, cycle detection rejects circular definitions fail-closed, and direct `BasedOn` references are mandatory in object manifests. SDK `ExportFile` XML automatically extracts domain and attribute definitions from object properties or metadata parts, binds `DomainBasedOn`/`AttributeBasedOn`/`ATTCUSTOMTYPE` (`dom:`/`att:`), and connects explicit `BasedOn` edges on Procedures and SDTs. Verified by 10 end-to-end integration tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/domain_attribute.rs` (procedure variable computations, chained domains, SDT field access, cycle and depth guards, and end-to-end `xml-run`). New `domain_attribute_projection` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer). Portable KB Catalog & XML Projection subsystem moves from 33.3% to 66.7% (4/6 areas: Procedures, SDTs, Domains, Attributes), and overall weighted SDK replacement moves from 37.0% to 40.7% (31/56 areas touched: 55.4%). Portable gate: 306 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds Structured Data Type (SDT) and Collection XML serialization and deserialization (`&sdt.ToXml([root])`, `&sdt.FromXml(&xml, [root])`, `&col.ToXml([root])`, `&col.FromXml(&xml, [root])`) matching official `GeneXus.Utils.GxUserType`, `GeneXus.Utils.GXBaseCollection<T>`, and generated C# (`type_Sdt*.cs`): `ToXml` produces indented multi-line XML with root element (defaulting to schema name or `"Collection"` for collections), self-closing tags for empty strings (`<Tag />`), XML entity escaping (`&lt;`, `&gt;`, `&amp;`, `&quot;`, `&apos;`), ISO date/datetime representations (`yyyy-MM-ddT00:00:00`), and boolean `true`/`false`; `FromXml` supports case-insensitive element matching, nested child SDT and collection deserialization, resetting missing fields to empty defaults (matching GeneXus SDK behavior), and returning `false` on malformed XML or mismatched root. Verified against 40 native `GxClasses.dll` oracle rows in `src/gx-engine/tests/fixtures/sdt_xml_native.tsv` and end-to-end compiled tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/sdt_xml.rs`. New `sdt_and_collection_xml` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer). Structured Data & Collections subsystem reaches 100.0% parity (5/5 areas), bringing all 3 core language subsystems (Language & Runtime Core 8/8, Control Flow 6/6, Structured Data & Collections 5/5) to 100.0% Native Parity (19/19 areas), and overall weighted SDK replacement moves from 35.0% to 37.0% (29/56 areas). Portable gate: 285+ tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds Structured Data Type (SDT) and Collection JSON serialization and deserialization (`&sdt.ToJson()`, `&sdt.FromJson(&json)`, `&col.ToJson()`, `&col.FromJson(&json)`) matching official `GeneXus.Utils.GxUserType`, `GeneXus.Utils.GXBaseCollection<T>`, and generated C# (`type_Sdt*.cs`): `ToJson` produces compact JSON with exact field casing and native representations (`"0000-00-00"` for empty Date, `"0000-00-00T00:00:00"` for empty DateTime, 0 for numeric, `""` for string, `false` for boolean, and JSON escape sequences); `FromJson` supports case-insensitive property matching, partial record updates (preserving unmentioned fields), array item repopulation via collection element prototype cloning, and fail-closed false return on invalid/malformed/non-container JSON without panicking. Verified against 32 native `GxClasses.dll` oracle rows in `src/gx-engine/tests/fixtures/sdt_json_native.tsv` and end-to-end compiled tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/sdt_json.rs`. New `sdt_and_collection_json` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer). Structured Data & Collections subsystem moves from 60.0% to 80.0% (4/5 areas), and overall weighted SDK replacement moves from 33.0% to 35.0% (28/56 areas). Portable gate: 283 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds short-circuit inline conditionals (`Iif(cond, trueExpr, falseExpr)`), universal empty/null state operations (`.IsEmpty()`, `IsEmpty(expr)`, `.SetEmpty()` on variables and record members, and `Nullvalue(&var)` across `Numeric`, `Text`, `Boolean`, `Date`, `DateTime`, `Collection`, and `Record`), `.UDP(...)` procedure calls, and `GeneXus.Utils.StringUtil` formatting/search/boolean utilities (`Format(pattern, v1..v9)`, `NewLine()`, `Replicate(str, count)`, `CharAt(str, idx)` / `.CharAt(idx)`, `StrSearchRev(s, find[, start])` / `.LastIndexOf(find[, start])`, `BoolToStr(b)`, and `ToBoolean(s)` / `StrToBool(s)` / `.ToBoolean()`). Verified against 57 native `GxClasses.dll` oracle rows in `src/gx-engine/tests/fixtures/conditional_state_native.tsv` and end-to-end compiled tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/conditional_state.rs`. New `conditional_and_state_operations` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer). Control Flow & Procedure Pipeline subsystem reaches 100.0% parity (6/6 areas), and overall weighted SDK replacement moves from 31.2% to 33.0% (27/56 areas). Portable gate: 280 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds local subroutine control flow (`Sub 'Name' ... EndSub` and `Do 'Name'`) matching native GeneXus C# generation (`protected void S111()` and `returnInSub`): subroutines are top-level, never executed on sequential fallthrough, share all procedure-scoped variables via zero-allocation mutable references (`struct P{index}Sub<'a>`), support case-insensitive name resolution and single/double/bare quote styles, enforce a recursion depth limit of 128 (`subroutine call depth limit exceeded`), and propagate early `Return` (both void and value-returning) to exit the enclosing procedure immediately. Verified by 13 end-to-end tests in `src/gx-engine/tests/subroutines.rs`. New `subroutine_control_flow` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer). Control Flow & Procedure Pipeline subsystem moves from 66.7% to 83.3% (5/6 areas), and overall weighted SDK replacement moves from 29.1% to 31.2%. Portable gate: 275 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds calendar and Date/DateTime operations and method equivalents matching the official `GeneXus.Utils.DateTimeUtil` runtime: `Day`, `Month`, `Year`, `Hour`, `Minute`, `Sec`/`Second`, `Dow` / `.DayOfWeek()`, `AddYr` / `.AddYears()`, `AddMth` / `.AddMonths()`, `DAdd` / `.AddDays()`, `TAdd` / `.AddSeconds()`, `.AddHours()`, `.AddMinutes()`, `DDiff`, `TDiff`, `Age` / `.Age()`, `EndOfMonth` / `.EndOfMonth()`, `ResetTime` / `.ResetTime()`, and `.ToString()` across all primitive types. Fixture `src/gx-engine/tests/fixtures/calendar_ops_native.tsv` (83 rows) verified against live .NET oracle and tested in `src/gx-engine/tests/calendar_ops.rs`. New `calendar_operations` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer).

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds collection mutating and inspection operations (`Add`, `Remove`, `Clear`, `IndexOf`), `For In` collection iteration, and loop `Exit` matching the official `GeneXus.Utils.GxSimpleCollection<T>`, `GeneXus.Utils.GXBaseCollection<T>`, and `GeneXus.Utils.IGxCollection` runtime: `&col.Add(&item [, &index])`, `&col.Remove(&index)`, `&col.Clear()`, `&col.IndexOf(&item)`, `For &item in &col ... EndFor`, and `Exit`. Semantics match ILSpy-decompiled .NET runtime: `Add(item, 0)` appends, `Add(item, idx > 0)` inserts at 1-based index `idx - 1`; `Remove(idx)` with `idx <= 0` or `idx > Count` is a safe no-op matching SDK; `IndexOf` returns 1-based index or 0 when absent; `For &item in &col` aliases elements allowing in-loop updates of collection elements; `Exit` immediately breaks out of the enclosing loop. Fixture `src/gx-engine/tests/fixtures/collection_ops_native.tsv` (24 rows) verified against live .NET oracle and tested in `src/gx-engine/tests/collection_ops.rs`. New `collection_operations` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer). Structured Data & Collections subsystem moves from 40.0% to 60.0% (3/5 areas), and Control Flow & Procedure Pipeline moves from 50.0% to 66.7% (4/6 areas). Portable gate: 259 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds mathematical and number conversion core functions and method equivalents matching the official `GeneXus.Utils.NumberUtil` and `GeneXus.Utils.StringUtil` runtime: `Int(val)` / `&val.Int()`, `Round(val, digits)` / `&val.Round(digits)`, `Trunc(val, digits)` / `&val.Trunc(digits)`, `Val(text)` / `&text.ToNumeric()`, `Left(text, size)` / `&text.Left(size)`, `Right(text, size)` / `&text.Right(size)`, `Asc(text)` / `&text.Asc()`, `Chr(ascii)`, `NotNumeric(text)` / `&text.NotNumeric()`, and `LTrimStr(val, len[, dec])`. Semantics match ILSpy-decompiled .NET runtime: `Int` uses `Math.Floor(value)`, `Round` uses `MidpointRounding.AwayFromZero` supporting negative digits for power-of-10 rounding, `Trunc` truncates towards zero supporting negative digits, `Val` trims whitespace and parses digits stopping at first non-digit character (returning 0 on empty/invalid), `Left` clamps negative size to 0, `Right` throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` on negative size, `Asc` returns 32 on empty string, `NotNumeric` returns false on empty string, and `LTrimStr` trims leading spaces from formatted `Str`. Fixture `src/gx-engine/tests/fixtures/numeric_math_native.tsv` (107 rows) verified against live .NET oracle and tested in `src/gx-engine/tests/numeric_math.rs`. New `numeric_math_functions` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer). Language & Runtime Core subsystem reaches 100% parity (8/8 areas). Portable gate: 256 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds extended text functions and method equivalents matching the official `GeneXus.Utils.StringUtil` runtime: `LTrim(text)` / `&text.TrimStart()`, `RTrim(text)` / `&text.TrimEnd()`, `PadL(text, size[, fill])` / `&text.PadLeft(size[, fill])`, `PadR(text, size[, fill])` / `&text.PadRight(size[, fill])`, `Space(count)`, `Concat(init, last[, sep])` / `&text.Concat(other[, sep])`, `StartsWith(text, prefix)` / `&text.StartsWith(prefix)`, `EndsWith(text, suffix)` / `&text.EndsWith(suffix)`, `Contains(text, needle)` / `&text.Contains(needle)`, and `StrSearch(text, find[, start])` / `&text.IndexOf(find[, start])`. Semantics match the ILSpy-decompiled .NET runtime: `LTrim`/`RTrim` trim only ASCII spaces (`' '`), `PadL` trims both ends before left-padding with truncation, `PadR` trims trailing spaces before right-padding, `Concat` trims trailing spaces of the first operand only, and `StrSearch` uses 1-based indexing with clamped start. Fixture `src/gx-engine/tests/fixtures/text_extended_native.tsv` (95 rows) verified against live .NET oracle and tested in `src/gx-engine/tests/text_extended.rs`. New `text_extended_functions` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer). Portable gate: 253 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean.

- **Experimental engine backend switch.** The Gateway can route operations to `gx-engine` instead of the GeneXus SDK behind an explicit gate: `Engine.Backend` / `Engine.Fallback` / `Engine.Executable` / `Engine.TimeoutSeconds` in `config.json` (or `GXMCP_ENGINE_*` in non-strict configurations; strict documents reject the overrides as structural) select the backend, and `EngineCapabilityRegistry` + `EngineRouter` refuse anything that is not at measured parity - with `Fallback=error` the call is refused (`EngineCapabilityNotParity`) instead of silently using the SDK. `genexus_doctor` now reports the resolved backend, the capability counts, the operations currently routed to the engine and, with `Backend=gxengine`, a live `gx-engine --version` probe (`executable` / `available` / `version` or a structured error). No product operation is routed yet: `str_format` is the only capability at parity and has no consumer, so default behavior is unchanged. `gx-engine` gained the `--version`/`-V` handshake. See `docs/engine_backend.md`.
- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` adds the measured GeneXus text functions `Substr(text, start, len)`, `Upper(text)`, `Lower(text)` and `StrReplace(text, find, replace)`, plus their method forms `.Substring(start, len)`, `.ToUpper()`, `.ToLower()` and `.Replace(find, replace)`. An SDK syntax scan pinned the accepted spellings; the rejected ones (`Substring(...)`, `Replace(...)`, `&v.Substr(...)`, `&v.Upper()`, `&v.Lower()`) stay unresolved-call errors. Semantics were measured in disposable KBTeste: `Substr` is 1-based over the raw stored value (explicit trailing spaces preserved; `Character` fields get no padding), `start < 1` or past the raw length yields empty text, a negative length takes the remainder and a longer length clamps; `Upper`/`Lower` map per character and keep the original when the mapping expands (`ß` stays `ß`, `µ` becomes `Μ`), with accented round-trips matching; `StrReplace` is case-sensitive, non-overlapping and left to right, an empty find is a no-op and an empty replacement removes. The 63 recorded rows live in `src/gx-engine/tests/fixtures/text_functions_native.tsv` and run through `src/gx-engine/tests/text_functions.rs` (fixture differential plus build/run e2e over both spellings). The postfix method grammar moved out of `parse_primary` into `parse_method` so the deepest expression-recursion frame keeps its stack budget: the nesting-limit test now passes on a 1.5 MiB thread stack where the pre-change parser needed more than 1.93 MiB. New `text_functions` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer; `routedOperations` unchanged). Portable gate: 250 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean. Not SDK replacement or native-KB parity.
- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` renders Date/DateTime values exactly as the measured native `&var.ToString()` does, and `Print` of a Date/DateTime value (variable or expression) is now admitted as that picture: `MM/dd/yy` with zero padding and the year modulo 100 (years 100 and 1900 render `00`, 9999 renders `99`), DateTime adds a 12-hour `hh:mm AM/PM` time with seconds dropped (`14:05:09` is `02:05 PM`, `00:30` is `12:30 AM`, noon is `12:00 PM`), empty values render `"  /  /  "` and `"  /  /   12:00 AM"`, and the measured empty-date-with-time case `YMDHMSToT(0, 0, 0, 14, 5, 9)` renders `01/01/01 02:05 PM` through the existing component override. The same discovery probes pinned the native conversion rules the engine keeps failing closed on: bare `msg(&numeric)`/`msg(&date)`/`msg(&dateTime)` are `spc0009` type mismatches and `&text = &date` is `spc0010`; text concatenation with a numeric uses raw C# concatenation (unpadded, decimal keeps full precision - `&p = &p / 3` prints 28 digits with the machine separator - and dates render culture-dependently), and a parenthesized arithmetic operand inside a `msg` concatenation generates invalid C# in 18.0.10 (the shape does not compile), so text concatenation with Date/DateTime and Numeric operands stays rejected and no culture-dependent bytes are claimed. Fixture `src/gx-engine/tests/fixtures/date_display_native.tsv` (18 recorded rows, machine-derived by `scratchpad/make-date-display-fixture.py`) runs through `src/gx-engine/tests/date_display_native.rs` (fixture differential plus a build/run e2e comparing raw stdout bytes). New `date_display` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer; `routedOperations` unchanged). Portable gate: 252 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean. Not SDK replacement or native-KB parity.
- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` SDK XML exchange projects deep SDT hierarchies recursively: nested Levels become per-level synthetic schemas with direct-child `Uses` edges, nested collections are admitted from `AttCollection`, and scalar-only Items fail closed. Type aliases stay evidence-gated: depth-1 children register `Root.Name`/`Root.Item`, depth-2 collections register the full-path item alias `Root.Child.Item` (the only name native KBTeste readback preserves beyond the root), and deeper collections or non-root composites stay unregistered. A captured three-level native export runs with an injected companion Procedure through `xml-run` with exact output (defaults, nested writes, collection count, item-alias write/read). Portable gate: 241 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean. Not SDK replacement or native-KB parity.
- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` (format 2) adds Numeric `For`/`EndFor` with literal `Step` (start evaluated once, `To` re-evaluated each iteration, mismatched direction runs zero times, per native KBTeste probes), `Len(text)`, `Str(numeric, width[, scale])` with native-matched overflow fill, numeric-expression `Collection.Item` indices, nested Record/Collection SDT projection from portable catalogs and SDK XML (one native nested export runs unchanged with matching output), direct-`Calls` validation, and `--diagnostics=json` phase categories (generated-program stderr captured, capped at 16 KiB). Portable gate: 236 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean. Not SDK replacement or native-KB parity.
- Experimental `xml-*` CLI compiles a bounded read-only projection of authored SDK `ExportFile` XML into the independent catalog/compiler. A fresh disposable-KB SDK export executed unchanged through Rust and matched all four native output lines; no SDK is loaded by the Rust path. Unsupported kinds, parts, module names, nested records, unresolved references and duplicate identities fail closed; XML bytes/nodes/depth are bounded with DTD/entity resolution disabled. This is not native KB decoding, XPZ extraction, lossless roundtrip or full SDK parity.
- Experimental format-2 scalar records now compile to standalone Rust: `new()`, Numeric member reads/writes, aliasing assignment, and scalar-field `Clone()` separation match a fresh disposable GeneXus oracle (`0, 9, 9, 9, 11`). Nine additional fresh native observations cover Character/Varchar field defaults, spaces, aliasing and Clone; twelve cover Boolean/Date/DateTime fields. Portable catalogs admit explicit `Uses` SDT dependencies with a bounded scalar `Fields` JSON part; schemas, type/storage bounds and incremental invalidation are validated. Void helpers admit same-schema record variables with native-backed `in`/`out`/`inout` entry, aliasing, rebinding and copyback behavior. Native KB SDT decoding, collections/nested records and record expression results remain unsupported.
- Experimental Rust control flow admits bounded `Do Case`, bare void `Return` (with `out`/`inout` copyback), and CLI text `Msg(..., status)`. Native Do Case fixtures verify first-match, fallthrough, nesting and skipped later helper conditions; other message modes, implicit conversion, and interactive UI behavior remain unsupported. Lowered case nesting is checked before build artifacts, and terminating branches no longer incorrectly participate in definite-assignment joins.
- `genexus_workwith` typed Selection filter authoring: `list_filters` reads the filter attributes and conditions of a Level (native SDK first, PatternInstance XML read-only otherwise) and `add_filter_attribute` adds a filter attribute with description, domain and conditions to standard WorkWith (WorkWith for Web) pattern instances through the native SDK only, returning a verified receipt with explicit `derivedObjectRegeneration: "not_guaranteed"` and supporting `dryRun` previews. `domain` is stored as given without validation; `defaultValue`, Domain validation and a persisted-part reread proof are left for a follow-up ([#559](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/559)).
- Raw `PatternInstance` XML (`genexus_edit part=PatternInstance` / `genexus_io import_part`) accepts reordering `attribute`, `variable`, `userAction` and the other leaf layout elements inside a WorkWithPlus `table`, `actionGroup`, `tabs` or `tab` container by element identity (reported as `Reorder`) instead of rejecting the moved child as an identity change, and accepts removing several leaf elements from one container in a single import. No typed `genexus_wwp` move action exists ([#545](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/545), [#560](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/560)).
- `genexus_wwp` `<textBlock>` and grid row userAction support: `add_user_action` supports row-level actions within `Grid` containers, and form table layouts permit `<textBlock>` control additions ([#551](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/551)).
- `genexus_wwp add_tab` on a form without `<tabs>` creates the container under the unique `TableContent` (then `TableMain`, or an explicit `containerName` path) instead of failing with `TabsContainerNotFound`, and `children[].type` accepts `attribute`: an attribute already in a form table is moved into the tab, element and XML attributes intact, and reported in `movedAttributes`; one not on the form is created ([#552](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/552)).
- Raw `PatternInstance` XML (`genexus_edit part=PatternInstance` / `genexus_io import_part`) accepts the DVelop Combo insert prompt: a `<prompt prompt="<guid>-<Trn>"><parameters><parameter name="..." /></parameters></prompt>` child may be added to or removed from an existing WorkWithPlus `<attribute>` (reported as `Insert`/`Remove` of `prompt`). No typed `genexus_wwp` action exists for it ([#560](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/560)).
- `genexus_workwith action=remove_action` allows atomic removal of custom actions from WorkWith and WorkWithDevices pattern instances, supporting typed SDK element deletion and XML fallback while preserving neighboring actions and validating post-save structure ([#538](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/538)).
- Transaction attribute formula indexing and search in `genexus_query` / `SourceSearchService`: attribute formulas defined at transaction and table level are indexed into `SearchIndex.IndexEntry.Formula` and searchable via `fields=["formula"]`, as well as exposed in `genexus_analyze` formula dependency analysis ([#533](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/533)).
- Experimental standalone `src/gx-engine` Rust compiler: validated portable JSON projects, a bounded numeric source frontend, semantic checks, native Rust emission, and a direct-rustc build/run CLI. Authored fixtures compile and execute without loading the GeneXus SDK or invoking MSBuild. This is an independently implemented subset, not native-KB interoperability or full GeneXus compatibility; it is not wired into production MCP/Worker compilation or provider fallback. See plan 129's implementation ledger for evidence and remaining milestones.
- Experimental Rust typed-program path (portable format 2): explicit Boolean/Numeric declarations, decimal literals and checked decimal arithmetic, strict type checking, storage distinct from display rounding/width, and native executable emission without SDK/MSBuild. A new Rust executable matches 19 source-matched native Boolean/Numeric observations; integer wrapping is a diagnosed safety divergence. Typed by-value helpers now support declared Boolean/Numeric returns, checked call boundaries, native call-hoisting semantics, shared runtime guards, and incremental explain with signature/declaration invalidation. Fresh native-vs-Rust helper execution matched 12 output lines; native probes at all integer precisions 1..18 established checked Int16/Int32/Int64 storage limits. Output/inout parameters and complete decimal/SDK compatibility remain unimplemented.

- Experimental Rust typed path: GeneXus `Parm` modes (`in:`, `out:`, `inout:`, unprefixed = inout), void helper Procedures, and call statements (`Proc(args)`, `Proc.Call(args)`). Copy-in/copy-back semantics, empty `out` on entry and last-write-wins aliasing match native KBTeste probes (Numeric; empty Boolean/VarChar/Date out values). Format 1 rejects modes and call statements.

- Experimental typed Character/Varchar storage, Unicode literals, concatenation, trailing-space-insensitive equality, UTF-16 `.Length()`, and text helper parameters/results. Native evidence has 17 matching observations and one explicit encoding-loss divergence: Rust preserves an emoji that the native CP1252-generated C# replaces with `??`. Per-value resource limits are checked; unsupported text operations fail closed. This is standalone procedure behavior, not database-column or full string-library parity.

### Changed

- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` implicit Numeric printing (`NumericType::render`) now delegates to the verified `Str` picture pipeline: native `&var.ToString()` was measured to be exactly `StringUtil.Str(value, precision, scale)` - padding included - over 120 KBTeste cases covering precision 1..18 boundary families, the scale corners (separator place, exact halves rounding past the picture, negative bounds), zero/negative zero and the width 15..18 narrowed-bound regime. The previous truncation-based rule diverged for scale > 0 (`(5,2)` 999.99 rendered instead of `*****`), widths 15..18 (narrowed bound) and padding. Recorded rows: `src/gx-engine/tests/fixtures/numeric_render_native.tsv` + `src/gx-engine/tests/numeric_render_native.rs`; probe-lane fixtures that recorded `.ToString().Trim()` (date/datetime, For, parm modes, void return, typed calls/do-case) now compare printed lines at value level with in-place provenance. New `numeric_render` capability at parity in `EngineCapabilityRegistry` (no consumer; `routedOperations` unchanged). Portable gate: 247 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean. Not SDK replacement or native-KB parity.
- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` `format_str` rendering is now single-allocation and `Wide::div_rem` takes a hardware fast path when both operands fit in 128 bits (every shape the picture pipeline can produce), replacing the 256-step bitwise loop; on the SDK-vs-engine benchmark corpus (6,152 rows) the engine went from 530 ns/row on the first recorded run to 121-130 ns/row and now measures ~2.25x the throughput of the native `GeneXus.Utils.StringUtil.Str` baseline (274-286 ns/row, medians of three 100-pass runs), with live parity unchanged (identical digest, 0 mismatches). Portable gate: 246 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean. Not SDK replacement or native-KB parity.
- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` `Str(numeric, width[, scale])` now reproduces the native picture rule for every admitted width 1..18 and scale 0..width, including the large-width regime: the asterisk bound `10^places - sign - 5*10^-scale` is built the way the GeneXus 18 `StringUtil.Str` runtime builds it (a `double`, narrowed through `(decimal)` to 15 significant digits), so widths 9..18 no longer diverge from the exact-rational ideal; rounding is half away from zero at the display scale, the integral zero renders only when a place exists, and a value that rounds to zero loses its sign. Evidence: 36,246 in-domain rows across six recorded native scans (including bound-boundary families and exact powers) with zero mismatches; `tests/fixtures/str_matrix.tsv` (6,152 rows) and `fixtures/str-bounds-native.tsv` (the native double/narrowing pair for every admitted width/scale/sign) are checked in. Portable gate: 246 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean. Not SDK replacement or native-KB parity.
- Experimental Rust `gx-engine` collection `Item` indices now accept fractional Numeric values and truncate them toward zero, matching native KBTeste (1.9 -> item 1, 2.7 -> item 2, 1.5 -> item 1); nonpositive or out-of-range results keep the engine's controlled diagnostic where native terminates with an unhandled `NullReferenceException` (documented safety divergence). The native differential also matched in-range variable, expression and For-driven indices exactly. Portable gate: 243 tests passed, 2 ignored; Clippy and rustfmt clean.
- `genexus_workwith action=add_action` preflight now validates procedure parameter arity against the target procedure's `parm()` rule, returning a structured `ArityMismatch` error response in both `dryRun` and real write while preserving signature advisories ([#537](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/537)).
- `genexus_edit` pattern instance preflight guidance for Work With for Web now directs to `genexus_workwith action=add_action` without conflating standard WorkWith with WorkWithPlus ([#536](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/536)).
- `genexus_analyze` call count scanner iterates `Events`, `Source`, and `Rules`, skipping `Source` when `Events` was already scanned to prevent duplicate caller counts on Transaction and WebPanel objects while preserving Procedure scans ([#528](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/528)).
- `genexus_edit` object-level save fast path and strict mode boundaries clarified in tool help catalog and documentation ([#534](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/534)).
- Synchronized and updated envelope documentation in `docs/envelope-coverage.md` for transaction, attribute, and pattern operations ([#521](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/521)).

### Fixed

- `genexus_wwp add_tab` review fixes for the Transaction tab synthesis (https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/552): an `attribute` child no longer adds a second `<attribute>` while the original stays in `TableAttributes` (it is moved, natively with `RemoveElementCommand` + `AddElementCommand`, and the post-save re-read fails closed and rolls back if the attribute is still on the form twice); the same attribute requested twice or found in two form tables is refused (`InvalidWwpChild`, `AttributeAmbiguousOnForm`); a form without `TableContent`/`TableMain` is refused with `TabsContainerNotFound` + `availablePaths` instead of placing `<tabs>` under the first `<table>` of the document, and two candidates are `TabsContainerAmbiguous`; the native `<tabs>` is created inside the same `ExecuteUpdate` as the tab; the tab projection, position and verification count every tab kind (`tab`, `gridTab`, `tabularTab`, `webComponentTab`) so a re-read that normalizes the element name no longer reports `WwpTabNotPersisted`; the `children.type` schema, help and capabilities inventory declare `attribute`. Still unverified on a real WorkWithPlus package: that `CreateChildElement("tabs")` under `TableContent` and `CreateChildElement("tab")` (the DVelop `TabsTransactionElement`/`TabTransactionElement` pair, rather than `tabularTab`) are accepted and projected into the Transaction WebForm, that a bare attribute name is accepted where the SDK stores `<typeGuid>-<Name>`, and that a re-parented native `attribute` element survives the save.
- `genexus_workwith` Selection filter authoring no longer reports a filter as applied without evidence: `add_filter_attribute` writes only through the native WorkWith SDK (a missing surface is refused with `WorkWithSdkUnavailable`, an SDK failure with `WorkWithFilterAuthoringFailed`, and the former raw-XML write fallback that the structural preflight always rejected is gone), resolves `SaveTo`/`AddCondition` before touching the instance, rereads the filter after saving and returns `route`, `verified`, `verificationScope` and `verification.observedSource: "in_memory_instance_reread"` (or `VerificationMismatch`). `list_filters` and the duplicate pre-check now read the real `filterAttribute`/`condition` nodes, so populated filters are no longer reported as empty when the XML path is used. Instances with two or more Levels require `level` (`FilterLevelAmbiguous` lists the candidates; unknown levels are `FilterLevelNotFound`, a Level without a selection is `WorkWithSelectionMissing`) and the response reports the Level used. Requests are validated before any write: identifier-shaped `attribute` (optional leading `&`), `FilterConditionEmpty` and `FilterConditionDuplicate` (within the request or against existing conditions); the dryRun plan no longer invents a description. `description` is now declared in the tool schema ([#559](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/559)).
- Raw `PatternInstance` XML edits of an `<attribute>`'s existing DVelop Combo `<prompt>` are accepted: adding or removing a `<parameter>` and changing the `prompt` target are reported as one `Update` of `prompt` instead of `PatternStructureChangeUnsupported ... Added: parameter`. A new or replaced `<prompt>` now requires a non-empty `prompt` (or `gxobject`) target, and one with an unknown attribute, a non-leaf `<parameter>` or a parent other than `<attribute>` is rejected with the offending item named ([#560](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/560)).
- `genexus_create` (`action=object` and `action=object_atomic`) preserves the object `description`, the SDT seed item's length/decimals from `firstItemType` (`_meta.seeded` now reports the size the SDK actually kept), and an Attribute's `dataType`/`length`/`decimals`/`basedOn` instead of discarding them. The Attribute path is fail-closed: a requested type that does not apply in memory returns `CreateMetadataNotApplied` (unknown Domain: `DomainNotFound`) before anything is saved, a post-save re-read reports `_meta.attribute` `{requested, applied, persistedVerified}` and returns `CreateMetadataNotPersisted` when the KB kept different metadata, and `dryRun` no longer echoes the object type as `dataType` when none was requested. Schema and help now document the Attribute inputs ([#541](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/541)).
- SDT member type changes on existing nodes: `genexus_structure action=update_visual mode=replace` names the member and the expected/observed field in `StructureUpdateFailed` (`mismatchDetail`, also in the message). A `genexus_edit part=Structure` write whose post-save re-read dropped part of the request (the SDK kept `VARCHAR(7)` for a requested `NUMERIC(7)`) now restores the pre-write structure and proves the restore by re-reading it: `rolledBack` is `true` only when the re-read matches, otherwise `rollbackError` says why; `unappliedMembers` lists the dropped DSL lines and the message no longer points at a retry or at EntityVersion drift. The patch receipt carries that evidence (`rollback.source="write-service"`) instead of calling the revert a normalization difference and does not stack a textual rollback on it. The SDK member type is written through the unambiguous `Type` property after the domain/attribute/ATTCUSTOMTYPE bindings are cleared; stale bindings are cleared and inline lengths/decimals applied. Whether the SDK accepts a primitive type change on an existing member is still unproven on a test KB; the fail-closed, verified-rollback receipt is what this entry guarantees ([#542](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/542)).
- `genexus_variable action=add|modify` with an SDT/Business Component type no longer reports a false `VariableTypeNotPersisted` when the SDK persisted the reference as an ATTCUSTOMTYPE EntityKey pair: the three readers of that pair (`VariableInjector`, `SdtDslParser`, `SdtMemberResolver`) share the new order-tolerant `CustomTypeReferenceParser` (`<Type>`/`<Id>` in either order, whitespace allowed) instead of two contradictory regexes. The post-save read-back reports how the object was resolved (`objectBinding.resolutionPath`: `native-reference`, `custom-type-name` or `data-type-name`) and a failed read-back carries `resolutionPath` plus the bounded `customType` text; a by-name resolution is reported, not promoted. The element order the SDK really emits still needs confirmation on a test KB ([#543](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/543)).
- Layout XML equivalence for WebForm and Report post-save verification tolerates what the SDK derives on persist: control `id` attributes it strips or regenerates (only for layout comparisons via `XmlEquivalenceOptions.Layout`; pattern instances, transfers and no-change detection keep `id` significant), `&var` references stored as `var:N`, and Report control `Name`/`ControlName` renamed after the `AttributeReference` (`ControlSource`), including when the persisted layout is a superset of the request. This avoids false `WriteNotPersisted` errors that led agents to re-apply layout writes and duplicate controls ([#544](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/544)).
- `genexus_edit mode=patch` receipts no longer label every divergence after a completed SDK save as a formatting difference: `WriteNotPersisted` now carries `mismatchKind` (`normalization` when the persisted text equals the request under the verifier's normalized or semantic canonicalization, `content` otherwise) with a message and recovery hint that never advise retrying an already-saved patch; `sdkSaveCompleted` is always exposed at the top of the receipt next to the fail-closed `saved` claim; and the `verifyMode` schema, tool help and playbook now state that `saved` in `mode=patch` is the verified-persistence claim, not the physical save ([#547](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/547)).
- `ReadDiagnosticHelper.CreateFailureEnvelope` no longer claims persistent log status at enqueue time: `enqueued=true` reports the hand-off to the asynchronous logger, `persisted` is `null` (unconfirmed) rather than `false` until a writer could confirm it, and `persisted=false` is reserved for a failed hand-off (`persistenceError` present); hints point directly to `error.diagnostics`, which carries the sanitized inner-exception chain the log summary omits ([#548](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/548)).
- `ReadDiagnosticSanitizer.SanitizeText` strips GeneXus code blocks, commands, and statements embedded in synthetic exception messages to prevent source leaks in read diagnostic telemetry; the statement rule now applies only to continuation lines and the command rule requires the parenthesis to follow the command name, so the first line of legitimate SDK messages (such as `Event 'Start' is not defined ...`) and prose like `call (RPC)` are no longer erased. Envelope, inner-exception, stderr, XML and safe-message regressions cover the sanitizer end to end ([#549](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/549)).
- `ReadDiagnosticSanitizer.SanitizeStackTrace` enforces the frame and character limits together under a hard ceiling: the sanitized stack trace, truncation markers included, never exceeds `MaxStackTraceLength`, and when both limits apply the output keeps both the length and the frame markers instead of letting long lines bypass the character bound ([#550](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/550)).
- WorkWithPlus Transaction authoring gaps behind the `<textBlock>`, grid row action and selection parameter rejections ([#551](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/551)): `genexus_wwp add_user_action` honours `position` on both the XML preview and the native `InsertElementCommand` path, counted among the container's standard and user actions so a grid row action can precede `Update`; a bare `containerName` no longer matches an unnamed table under the named one (`TableMain` with an anonymous responsive child reported `FormActionContainerAmbiguous`), only the unnamed grid answers to `Grid`; `genexus_io import_part part=PatternInstance` accepts adding or removing `<parameter name value/>` leaves inside an existing `<parameters>` of a `selection`, `prompt` or `userAction`, and a new `<parameters>` list under `<selection>`; the structural preflight compares untouched siblings by name, attribute set and significant children instead of `XNode.DeepEquals`, so an export re-serialised by the client (attribute order, indentation, `<x></x>`) no longer rejects a well-formed `<textBlock>` as `1 child element added`, and a sibling that really changed is named in the rejection. Schema and help document `containerName: Grid` and the `position` convention. Native persistence of a selection parameter list and of the inserted row position remains unverified on this host.
- `SourceStoreBackfillService` no longer lets a per-object read failure consume the transient slice retry budget (a run of five unreadable objects previously parked the walk in `error` at `retryCount: 5` until the next index trigger), keeps `readFailedObjects` across Worker restarts and re-walks so a deterministically unreadable object is skipped without an SDK read until the catalogue signature changes, caps the GUIDs reported through `genexus_whoami` and read-diagnostic snapshots at 50 (`failedObjects`/`failedCount` carry the total), and treats `completeWithFailures` as certified coverage for the in-memory FullSource promotion budget ([#553](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/553)).
- `SourceStoreBackfillService` maintains linear counts for processed, stored, and stale objects per iteration instead of cumulatively adding slice-local counters, preventing triangular counter inflation; `removedObjects` is reset together with the other counters when the catalogue changes; `triggerState` is no longer reloaded from the persisted snapshot, so after a Worker restart it follows the live `state` instead of freezing on the value persisted before the restart; and the `etaMs` rate is measured only over the objects timed by the current run clock, which is reset with the counters, so a persisted count no longer deflates the ETA after a restart and a previous run no longer inflates it after a catalogue reset ([#554](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/554)).
- `GraphToolsService.Resolve` includes `type` in the `AmbiguousName` message and hint of `genexus_impact` / `genexus_object_context` only when a non-empty type value identifies exactly one candidate; a type-less candidate no longer produces a placeholder `type=` hint, and the `genexus_impact` help now states that `type` disambiguates ([#555](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/555)).
- `genexus_lifecycle action=rebuild` is now consistently KB-wide across contract, preview and code. The Gateway intercept refuses a `rebuild` carrying `target` with `TargetNotSupported` on every path (stdio, HTTP, synchronous, async and `dryRun`) before any Worker dispatch, and the Worker refuses a `RebuildAll` carrying a target as defense in depth; the targetless `dryRun` preview reports `wouldBuild: ["<entire selected Knowledge Base>"]` like `build_all` instead of an empty list; the tool help, the schema description and the docs no longer claim that `rebuild` "remains compatible with directed targets". This supersedes the #359 contract under which `rebuild target=<object>` was routed to the Worker's targeted `Rebuild`: that Gateway mapping became unreachable once the refusal landed and has been removed, with its tests retargeted to the refusal. `action=build target=<object>` is the directed path; the Worker's targeted `Rebuild` action remains reachable only through the legacy `genexus_build` route ([#546](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/546)).
- `genexus_io import_part part=PatternInstance` / `genexus_edit` on a WorkWithPlus instance: sibling reordering is now matched by identity case-insensitively (`controlName`/`ControlName`, `name`/`Name`, `attribute`, `variable`, and a unique `title` for an unnamed group `<table>`), so moving an `<attribute>` inside `TableAttributes` or a `<gridTab ControlName=...>` inside `<tabs>` is accepted instead of being refused as `identity changed at .../attribute/@attribute`; when a sibling has no identity, the rejection names that sibling and its index and says what to do instead of reporting a positional identity change; removing a leaf that has no identity attribute no longer escapes the preflight with an `ArgumentNullException`; and the rejection hint, the `genexus_edit` help and the `edit_pattern_instance` recipe now describe what raw PatternInstance XML accepts (property changes, one leaf added per container, several leaf removals, a leaf moved between containers, reordering by identity, a `<prompt>` under an `<attribute>`) and what it still refuses, instead of "limited to existing property changes". Removing and reordering inside the same container still takes two imports ([#545](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/545)).
- Nexus IDE test runner (`src/nexus-ide/src/test/runTest.ts`) hardened against the low-frequency `Nexus IDE checks` flake, whose cause remains unknown: every run launches VS Code with its own `--user-data-dir`/`--extensions-dir` pair under `src/nexus-ide/.vscode-test`, the pair is removed after a green run (set `NEXUS_IDE_KEEP_TEST_PROFILE=1` to keep it) and preserved after a failure, and a failed run logs `cwd`, `argv`, the launch arguments, the runtime paths and the Electron exit code/signal so it can be compared with a green run. The per-run profile isolates concurrent runs from the shared-profile `claimInstance` abort and stops the cache directory from growing; it does not claim to eliminate the flake ([#556](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/556)).
- `genexus_edit` no longer declares `"default": "normalized"` for `verifyMode` in its schema: clients that materialize schema defaults sent that value explicitly, so an omitted `verifyMode` on a `mode=ops` Structure write reached the Worker as `normalized` and `add_attribute` without a description still reported `WriteNotPersisted` over the attribute description the SDK derives from the name. The schema and tool help now describe the part-dependent default (Structure → `semantic`, text parts → `normalized`); an explicit `normalized`/`exact` on Structure that differs only by that description keeps its verdict but reports `error.mismatchKind: "sdk-generated-description"` with a hint; the unreachable `mode=ops` clause in the full-write facade default was removed; and the generated-description match accepts the SDK's word boundaries for a leading capital run (`ZTstMcpArq` → "ZTst Mcp Arq") ([#557](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/557)).
- `genexus_edit mode=ops` Structure rollback drops a Transaction attribute through the level's native Items storage (the path `genexus_structure action=remove_attribute` uses) instead of ending with `SDK-API-UNSUPPORTED`, marks the Structure part dirty so the restore save carries the removal, matches the name fallback only against items of the attribute's own kind (a homonym sub-level is never removed), and keeps `saved`, `rolledBack` and the messages telling one story: the Structure abort message says the write was aborted before its save step, `rollback.error` is prefixed `Rollback write failed:`, and the `mode=ops` note no longer says nothing was persisted after a save. The Items path and the messages are unit-tested with fakes; native proof that the removal is persisted after a rollback (re-read without the attribute, then `remove_attribute` answering `AttributeNotFound`) is still pending in a scratch KB ([#558](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/558)).
- Worker ownership records now include the gateway start time, preventing a reused PID from falsely blocking the same checkout/KB after a gateway exits. Acquisition and reconciliation check process identity; legacy records without timestamps remain conservatively reserved while their PID is alive. Synthetic-record regressions cover stale, live and legacy owners without opening a KB or killing a process.
- `genexus_analyze mode=impact`: when the search index contains no callers for an object, the analyzer now falls back to native SDK cross-references (`TryGetSdkCallers`) and flags `fieldTrust="partial:sdk-fallback"` instead of returning an incomplete caller graph ([#527](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/527)).
- `genexus_edit` semantic ops (`mode="ops"`) now properly forwards caller-provided `verifyMode` through to `WriteObject`, and defaults `Structure` part verification to semantic mode when unspecified so SDK-generated attribute descriptions do not cause false verification failures ([#525](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/525)).
- `genexus_edit` semantic ops (`add_attribute`, `set_attribute`, `remove_attribute`) over multiline formulas now accurately determine transaction DSL entry boundaries using bracket depth, preventing truncation of multiline formula definitions ([#526](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/526)).
- `genexus_lifecycle action=cancel` now accurately tracks child process states (`running`, `terminated`, `exited`, `unknown`), performs a bounded wait for child process exit before reporting final status, and fixes status resolution in external MSBuild runner phases ([#535](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/535)).
- `GxwireCacheLocation` root KB detection now treats `knowledgebase.connection` as a hard marker and excludes root temporary/metadata XML files (`CategoryViewConfig`, `iar_objs`, `nav_objs`) from triggering false root inferences ([#529](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/529)).
- `gxwire` lexer properly identifies chained method calls with leading dots (`.link(...)`, `.call(...)`, etc.) across newlines and boundary prefixes ([#530](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/530)).
- `gxwire` entity kind identification supports XML exports with custom namespaces and nested folder elements, resolving object kind via GUID mapping and folder fallbacks ([#531](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/531)).
- `gxwire` reference extraction captures Procedure and object calls nested within complex XML formula definitions and formula properties across multiline expressions ([#532](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/532)).
- In-process batch builds pass `ContinueOnError | BuildProcess | BuildCalled` (14561) again. Commit 449b67c4 had replaced it with `0x01|0x04|0x08` (BuildCalled | CompileMains | OnlyThemes), so called objects were not specified, generated or compiled.
- `genexus_create action=object_atomic` types SDT variables through the SDK's own type parser, which stores a resolved structure reference. A Procedure created in the same call that read `&Var.Member` failed with src0216 ("invalid property") and was rolled back. Verified live in KBTeste with `scripts/tests/live-sdt-member-access.py`, which fails with the old binding.
- Terse error envelopes keep `fieldErrors`, so `AtomicCreateValidationFailed` now shows which payload field was rejected, as its hint says.

### Internal

- The experimental Rust engine now has an SDK-vs-engine benchmark: `scripts/benchmark.py` runs one recorded corpus through the real `GeneXus.Utils.StringUtil.Str` runtime (`scripts/sdk-oracle`, official `genexus.classes.core` package) and through the engine's `format_str` bench target, re-checking live output parity (row count, character count, FNV-1a/UTF-16 digest) before reporting per-pass timings. First recorded run: 6,152 rows with identical output, 284 ns/row native vs 530 ns/row engine. Reports are measurements under the ignored scratchpad, not committed evidence.
- Experimental Rust engine test-helper temp directories now include a per-process sequence in `collection_indices` and `for_loop`, so pid+nanosecond-only roots can no longer collide and let one test delete a parallel test's in-flight build tree on machines with coarse `SystemTime` ticks.
- Asynchronous partial indexing synchronization in `IndexCacheService` triggers backfill on unscoped search and signals `OnIndexReady` lifecycle events to synchronize cache state with object service consumers ([#522](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/522)).
- Local cache and durable source store isolation (`DurableSourceStoreRoot`) is scoped per-KB (`GXMCP_KB_ID`), normalized path hash, and host generation, preventing transient path collisions and cleaning up orphaned stores ([#523](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/523)).
- Source store backfill service properly detects deleted objects during synchronization, removing stale object records from the persistent store and updating progress metrics ([#524](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/524)).
- The experimental Rust engine also includes a transactional incremental parse session and `explain` command: unchanged procedure bodies are reused, affected callers propagate through old/new graphs, and invalid revisions preserve the last accepted state. Full semantic validation and native compilation still run; no executable cache or measured SDK speedup is claimed.
