Two consequences, and the second is why it survived a reapply and a targeted build: the generated objects were **not reported**, and - because the same lookup drives the search-index registration right below it - they were **not added to the index** either, so they stayed invisible until something else reindexed the KB. The apply had worked all along; the report and the index were wrong. Both conventions are still probed, because a KB generated by an older release holds prefix-named objects, and the suffix candidates are ordered longest-first because `WWGetFilterData` and `WWExport` both begin with `WW` - strip `WW` first and the base comes back as `TbandadGetFilterData`, which is a lookup for a host that does not exist. The same wrong assumption made the `EditingWebFormUnderPattern` shadow warning never fire on a real generated object: it stripped a prefix that a suffix-named object does not have, so the one class of object where warning the author that the IDE will overwrite their edits mattered most was silently exempt. Related to [#349](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/349).

- When the generated family is still incomplete, the apply says so instead of reporting a bare success. The reporter's expectation was "generate the objects, or fail instead of `PatternApplied`", and failing hard is the wrong half of that: the generator registers new objects asynchronously after Invoke returns, so a member missing at that instant may be seconds from existing, and turning that race into a hard failure would reject applies that succeed. What is not acceptable is a success envelope that silently omits part of its own result, because it reads identically whether the family was generated, is still pending, or was never generated - and the first two are the common cases. The response now carries `expectedFamilyObjects`, `familyMissing`, `familyComplete` and a note saying which of the two situations this is and how to tell them apart. No verdict is asserted; the caller gets the names and decides.

- `genexus_lifecycle action=rebuild target=<objeto>` no longer rebuilds the entire Knowledge Base. Issue #359: the Gateway mapped `rebuild` to the Worker's `RebuildAll` unconditionally, and the Worker's whole-KB branch emits `<BuildAll/>` without ever reading `targets` - so the target was discarded rather than refused, the caller asked for one object and paid for the whole KB, and the response came back `Action: RebuildAll` with nothing indicating the substitution. The Worker's plan builder already emits `SpecifyOneOnly` + `ForceRebuild=true` for a targeted `Rebuild`; it was simply never asked to. A targeted rebuild now routes there, and targetless `rebuild` still routes to `RebuildAll` as the tool description promises. The Worker also refuses a `RebuildAll` carrying a target outright, as `BuildAll` already did, so any other caller is told rather than quietly given a KB-wide build. Two existing tests had the defect encoded as their expectation - `BuildCommandFactory_UsesRebuildAllForRebuild` asserted `RebuildAll` for `rebuild target=Customer` - and were rewritten to the corrected contract, with the targetless and whitespace-target branches pinned alongside.

- A rejected pattern structural edit now names what actually changed, and the `childrenOrderedList` message stops pointing at an action that does not exist. Issue #349 reported `PatternStructureChangeUnsupported: Child structure changed at /instance/[0]/...` for adding a `<textBlock>` that touched nothing but itself, and `PatternMetadataChangeUnsupported ... @childrenOrderedList` for a move between containers. Both messages were true and unusable. The second is the more interesting one: the reporter had completed the move correctly *by the wrong rule*, updating both `childrenOrderedList` values because that is what the attribute looks like it wants. It does not - the SDK rebuilds that list from the children on save, which is exactly why `PatternChildOrderReconciler` is deliberately kept out of the write path (`PatternReconcileAttachmentTests` pins that). The message now says so and tells the caller to leave the attribute out. Structural rejections also now name the offending attribute and the permitted set, instead of reporting a path. Related to [#349](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/349).

- Adding, moving and removing a WorkWithPlus layout element is now a permitted structural edit, which is the remaining manual step #349 asks for. Adding a leaf layout element to an existing container, removing one, and moving one between sibling containers are all accepted by the PatternInstance preflight, following the precedent the gate already set for `gridVariable` into a grid and `webComponent` into a table. **Cross-container moves need no special handling**: a move is one parent losing a child and a sibling gaining one, each already an independently permitted single-leaf operation, and the SDK derives both order lists from the resulting document order - so a matched insert+remove is additionally reported as the single `Move` it is. Every change records that ordering is not authored. Deliberately narrow, because this cannot be verified against a real PatternInstance without the WorkWithPlus package installed and the cost of being too permissive is a corrupt pattern rather than a rejected edit: exactly one node of difference, the node must be a leaf of a permitted kind (`textBlock`, `variable`, `attribute`, `image`, `userControl`, `actionGroup`, `errorViewer`), and every attribute must be in that kind's allow-list. Nested additions and container-valued additions stay refused, because their generated layout cannot be predicted from the XML alone. `gridAttribute` and `gridVariable` are **excluded on purpose** - they are already governed more narrowly by the grid authoring mode, and listing them here would have widened that silently, since the unconditional structural handler is consulted first. That regression happened during this work and is now pinned by a test. **Not verified:** persistence against a live WorkWithPlus PatternInstance. The package is not installed on this machine, so the preflight and its reporting are verified and the SDK round-trip is not. Related to [#349](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/349).

- `genexus_sdk_probe mode=surface` works again. The index builder did `fn.Substring((ns.Key + ".").Length)`, and a global-namespace type reports its namespace as the literal `"(global)"` - so 8 characters were stripped off a name that could be shorter than that, throwing `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` and failing the whole probe with `SdkProbeError`. The prefix is now stripped only when it is actually there. Found while investigating #351, where it blocked the one tool whose stated purpose is to make exactly this kind of investigation cheap.

- [#351](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/351) is answered: there **is** a typed SDK route for authoring a `filterAttribute` and its conditions in a standard WorkWith, and it is not XML surgery. `Artech.Patterns.WorkWith.FilterElement` exposes `AddFilterAttribute(String)`, `AddCondition(String)`, `FindFilterAttribute(String)`, `FindCondition(String)` and `DefineVariables(VariablesWrapper)`; `WorkWithInstance.Load(PatternInstance)` / `SaveTo(PatternInstance)` bracket the round trip; `FilterAttributeElement` carries settable `Name`, `Description`, `Domain`, `Default`, `AllValue` and `Prompt`. The shipped `WorkWithInstance.xml` schema agrees with the XML in the issue node for node, with `attributes` and `conditions` both `Optional="false"` under `Filter` - so the typed API and the serialized document are two views of one model. Navigation is `Levels[i].Selection.Filter`, and the fact that the selection hangs off the **Level** rather than the Transaction is exactly the multi-level ambiguity the issue anticipated: `FindLevel(id)`, `FindFilterAttribute(name)` and the per-element `InternalPath` are the disambiguating handles. The design consequence is that the right shape for this is a small typed operation rather than relaxing the XML gate, which would widen a raw-payload hole to cover a node the SDK already offers typed methods for, and would lose the duplicate-name and domain-reference validation those methods make free. Findings and the full member listing are in [`docs/sdk-probe/workwith-filter-authoring.md`](docs/sdk-probe/workwith-filter-authoring.md). **Not verified:** that `SaveTo` persists and re-reads as intended - no KB under `C:\KBs` has a standard WorkWith applied, and mutating a user's KB is not authorized. No implementation is included and none is claimed; the issue asked for feasibility, and this answers it.

  A correction to an earlier investigation note, since it changes what is reproducible where. The pattern packages are under the **major** directory - `GeneXus18\Packages\Patterns` - not `GeneXus\Packages\Patterns`, and the standard WorkWith pattern is installed there. `WorkWithPlus` is a DVelop package and is genuinely absent from this machine, so [#349](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/349) and [#359](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/359) remain unreproducible here while #351 was fully answerable.

  #359's second symptom is **not** addressed and not claimed: a build after `genexus_worker_reload mode=soft` regenerating the WorkWith with unresolved user controls (lost `.InternalName` references, dropped user-control attributes in the emitted `.cs`). Two hypotheses were checked and both are wrong, which is why nothing was changed for it. The first was a stale MCP-side template catalogue surviving the reload - but soft reload calls `Environment.Exit` and the Gateway respawns, so no static state survives it; a fix built on that premise was written and then reverted rather than shipped. The remaining hypothesis is the reporter's own: the fresh worker does not have the WorkWithPlus/DVelop package catalogue loaded, which is a GeneXus generator input rather than an MCP-side state, and it is consistent with this machine not having the package at all. Resolving it needs a machine with WorkWithPlus installed.

### Internal

- `docs/environment_variables.md` now documents the 46 environment variables the code read and the page did not, and a test stops the next one from landing undocumented. The omissions were not random: the five TeamDev credentials (`GXMCP_TEAMDEV_URL/USER/PASSWORD/TOKEN/AUTHTYPE`) were absent, so the one way to supply a TeamDev password without putting it in a tool argument - and therefore out of an agent transcript - was undiscoverable; so were the watchdogs that force-fail a wedged job (`GXMCP_INDEX_NO_PROGRESS_SEC`, `GXMCP_ASYNC_JOB_WATCHDOG_S`, `GXMCP_IDLE_GC`), which is what an operator with a stuck build searches for. Also newly documented: the advisory write lock and the two KB destination pins (`GXMCP_WRITE_OWNER_ID`, `GXMCP_WRITE_FORCE`, `GXMCP_EXPECTED_KB_PATH`, `GXMCP_EXPECTED_KB_VERSION`), the legacy structural transport overrides, the GeneXus install/KB path names, and the MTA executor capacities - the last of which no scan of a literal `GetEnvironmentVariable` call can see, because the name is an argument to a helper. Each section now states whether it is operator-facing, harness/test-only or third-party passthrough, and the harness switches are grouped in their own section so the operator-facing list stays short enough to read. No secret value appears anywhere on the page: the credential rows name the variable and the credential type only. The page's own unenforced "add a row here" note is replaced by `EnvironmentVariableDocCoverageTests`, which enumerates the read names under `src/` and fails when one is missing. It carries an explicit allowlist of the nine Gateway-injected names, each with a reason, and requires an allowlisted name to be documented under "Set internally (do not set by hand)" - an injected value presented as an operator knob invites someone to pin it, so the allowlist records intent rather than excusing a name. The test states its own limit instead of claiming exhaustiveness: it resolves literal arguments and `const`-bound names, and the three shapes it cannot resolve are named on the page, including the one genuinely runtime-chosen name (a `DataStoreAlias` credential reference, where the profile decides the variable name). Documentation and one test file only; no behaviour, config surface or tool schema change.
- The README's release section now describes the release that actually ships. It claimed the workflow triggers on a push to `main` with a `package.json` version bump, that it creates a GitHub Release tagged `v<version>`, and that a required `NPM_TOKEN` secret must be configured. None of the three is true of `.github/workflows/release.yml`: its only triggers are `release: [published]` and a manual `workflow_dispatch` with a `tag`, the job verifies the assets of a release that already exists rather than creating one, and its publish step unsets `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`/`NPM_TOKEN` and strips `_authToken` from the runner npmrc because npm auth is OIDC Trusted Publishing (`permissions: id-token: write`). An operator following the old text would have gone looking for a workflow that does not exist, and would have configured a secret the release deliberately scrubs. The section now names `release.ps1` as the entry point that builds, tags and creates the release, points at `docs/release_protocol.md` for the protocol, and states the OIDC arrangement. `cli/docs.test.js` asserts all three corrections through a new `markdownSection` locator addressed by the exact heading line, so the negative `NPM_TOKEN` guard covers the release section rather than the whole file - a legitimate mention elsewhere (a token for reading private packages, say) must not fail the build, and whole-file scoping would have forced someone to delete an accurate statement instead. Not a repository change, so flagged for the maintainer: if an unused `NPM_TOKEN` secret exists in the repository settings it should be removed there.

## v3.9.4 - 2026-09-29

### Tracked issues

- [#326](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/326) — [Bug] Redação de credenciais: under-masking por espaço no canônico e classes ausentes nos demais sítios
- [#327](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/327) — [Bug] collect-diagnostics.ps1 não redige credenciais, e o bundle é destinado a issue pública
- [#328](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/328) — [Chore] release.ps1 deixa ## Unreleased sem subseções após promover, favorecendo entradas arquivadas na versão errada
- [#329](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/329) — [DX] Erro "Process smoke binaries could not be bound" não indica causa nem remédio
- [#330](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/330) — [Bug] genexus_apply_pattern (WorkWithPlus) returns PatternNoOp on any WebPanel; mode=diagnose said all checks passed

### Fixed

- `genexus_apply_pattern pattern=WorkWithPlus` on a `WebPanel`/`WebComponent`/`SDPanel` no longer collapses every failure into one unservable sentence, and `mode=diagnose` no longer contradicts it. On a non-Transaction target the engine's `ApplyPattern` is a silent no-op — dead end #1 in `docs/sdk-probe/wwp-projection-discovery.md` — so the `PatternInstancePackageInterface` pipeline is the only route that can work, and every one of its stages can fail for a different reason. Diagnose ran none of them and still answered `All pre-apply checks passed. The pattern should apply cleanly.`, so a target that could never apply was advertised as ready; the apply then reported `Engine ApplyPattern void overload no-op'd on this target, and the WWP package's CreatePatternInstanceWithTemplate fallback also failed: ValidateAndSave returned false`, which names neither the template, the package, nor the projection. A new `WwpAttachPreflight` resolves every member the attach invokes — the helpers type, the `CreatePatternInstanceWithTemplate` overload for the specific parent type, `SetPatternApplyOnSave`, `ValidateAndSave`, and the `WorkWithPattern` → `GetBuildProcess` → `UpdateParentObject` projection chain — and both the attach and `mode=diagnose` run it, so they cannot disagree. Diagnose reports each blocking stage as a `critical` finding carrying `stage`, `detail` and the rejected resolution candidates, and the `ok` finding is suppressed whenever one exists. The apply returns a stable `failureStage` (`wwpAssembly`, `packageInterface`, `createOverload`, `template`, `projection`, `hostConflict`, `validateAndSave`, `attachVerification`, `environment`) plus a machine-readable `failureCode`, an `attachPreflight` payload, and a hint and next-steps keyed to that stage — `Update the WorkWithPlus pattern package` for an absent API, `genexus_delete_object` for a host conflict, `genexus_list_objects typeFilter="WorkWithPlus for Web Template"` for a missing template — instead of one blanket "try a different template" that cannot help in five of those cases. Related to [#330](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/330).

- A `WorkWithPlus` attach that fails now removes the host it created, and no longer adopts a host it did not. The `WWP_ApplyTemplate` MSBuild task — a documented headless dead end whose constructor needs an MSBuild engine host — ran *ahead* of the verified `PatternInstancePackageInterface` route on every `WebPanel` apply; when it did execute it could leave a `WorkWithPlus<X>` behind that the official create can then only fail on by name collision, and a `ValidateAndSave` run against that foreign host returns false. It is now a last resort, after the official route, and anything it leaves behind is cleaned up. The official route additionally refuses to adopt a pre-existing `WorkWithPlus<X>`: it reports `PatternHostAlreadyExists` and points at `genexus_delete_object` or `reapply=true` instead. This was self-poisoning. A failed attach left the host on disk with no binding, the next call's stale-host probe saw the host, took the reapply route, skipped the engine apply and projected an empty `PatternInstance` — the exact reported downstream symptom of a screen that renders while its buttons never reach the server. Every failure after the host exists (validation, projection, attach verification) now runs the fail-closed cleanup through `SdkDeletionAdapter`, reports `removedOrphanHost` when it succeeded and an actionable `orphanHostWarning` when it did not, and the projection step is no longer best-effort: a host that was created but never projected is a failure, not a success. `WwpProjectionHelper` populates `Failure` on every early return (it previously returned `false` with a null reason on nine paths), and the WWP assembly/type resolution it shared with the attach is now one `WwpPackageSurface.Resolve()` so the two cannot pick different assemblies.

- `genexus_orient` suggestions no longer describe something orient does not do. v2.7.0 folded orient as a duplicate of `genexus_whoami` and withdrew it from advertisement, but the dispatch path stayed live and several envelopes kept pointing at it with copy claiming it "shows the tool catalog" (Worker `UnknownMethodOrAction`/`UnknownAction` next-steps) or "lists each tool's input schema" (Gateway `InvalidArgs` next-steps). `OrientService.Welcome` returns `{kb, recentEdits, gotchas, topTools}` - a welcome card, not a catalog - so an agent following the suggestion arrived expecting a schema list. Every orient `why` now reads "Welcome card: KB info, recent edits, and top gotchas.", pinned by a source-scan regression guard. The dispatcher hint that also pointed at `genexus_help action=route` (not a published tool) now points at orient only, and the `genexus_whoami` update-available suggestion that passed a `topic` arg `Welcome` ignores is removed - the update status already ships in whoami's own payload.

- A credential redaction is no longer able to keep the tail of a secret. The canonical redactor read a value as a run of non-space characters, so it stopped at the first space and the rest of the value survived into a persisted preflight phase log, a publication error, an integration-preflight summary, and the diagnostics bundle: `password=my secret passphrase` became `password=[REDACTED] secret passphrase`, `authorization: Bearer abc def` kept `abc def`, and a quoted value lost everything after its first word. A value is now read as a unit - a quoted value is one unit whatever it contains, and an unquoted value runs to the first structural boundary: `,`, `;`, `&`, `|`, a newline, or the next `name=value` pair. The boundary is a deliberate over-masking limit, pinned by tests: `token=x foo=bar` keeps `foo=bar` so diagnostics stay readable, while unpaired prose on the same line is masked with the value, because the boundary between "more secret" and "more sentence" is not recoverable from the text. The literal token prefixes, the word-boundary rule that keeps `usertoken=x` untouched, and a single `[REDACTED]` marker for every credential class (including `user id`, which used a second marker) are all covered.

- Three sites that had their own narrower pattern set now delegate to the canonical one instead of drifting from it. `verify-release-publication.ps1` masked only `token|password|secret`, so a publication error carrying `pwd`, `api_key`, `authorization` or a connection string was persisted unmasked; `integration-preflight.ps1` covered no `api_key`, no `authorization` and none of the `ghp_`/`github_pat_`/`npm_` literal prefixes, and measured `api_key=sk-abc`, `authorization: Bearer abc` and `ghp_ABCDEF123456` passing through with no mask at all. Both keep their own caps (600 characters for the publication message) and no longer carry a local copy. Related to [#326](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/326).

- The diagnostics bundle no longer carries a credential to a public issue. `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.md` instructs the reporter to paste the output of `scripts/collect-diagnostics.ps1`, and that bundle includes the crash ledger plus the `[WORKER-CRASH]`/`[COLD-START]`/`[TOOL-LATENCY]`/`WorkerNativeCrashRecovered` log lines - all of which went through a `Redact` function that masked KB paths, home, user and host, and had no credential pattern whatsoever. The collector now applies the canonical credential redaction on top of its own identity redaction, and the script header, the bundle header and the issue template all say so. Related to [#327](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/327).

- A release no longer leaves `## Unreleased` as a heading with no subsection under it. The promotion inserted the version heading by pure insertion, so the old body moved under the version and the fresh `## Unreleased` was left bare; that state passes the "the heading disappeared" guard silently, and the natural next edit - appending a bullet to the first visible `### Fixed` - files new work under the release that was just published. The promotion now writes an explicit anchor into the fresh section: an HTML comment naming the contract, plus empty `### Added` / `### Changed` / `### Fixed` / `### Internal` headings. The anchor is deliberately not substantive, so the guard that refuses to ship an empty release - which now counts a line only when it is not blank, not inside an HTML comment, and not a bare heading - still fails an untouched anchored section. Related to [#328](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/328).

- The process-smoke binding failure now says what to do. `Process smoke binaries could not be bound to the current publish artifacts.` was one sentence for three different causes, and named neither the files compared nor the fix, so the operator had to infer both. The message now lists each compared path with its actual state - missing publish artifact, missing source build output, or a SHA-256 pair that does not match - states the probable cause for that specific state, and names the remedy (`.\build.ps1`). The dominant real-world case gets its own explanation: `publish\` built before a commit, where the SDK's revision stamp makes the rebuilt `src\` binary differ byte-for-byte. Related to [#329](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/329).

### Fixed

- A snapshot restore could report success while writing nothing. `WwpActionService`'s restore check was `Equals(Sha256(restored), Sha256(before))` and its `Sha256` returned null for a null input, so when neither side produced readable content the comparison evaluated null-equals-null, was true, and the response carried `patternRestoredExactly: true` and `exact: true` for a snapshot that was never restored. The comparison now rejects a missing side outright: a restore check that cannot see a difference must not certify one. Two sibling copies of the same hash, in `TextPersistenceVerifier` and `EventsSaveIsolation`, hashed null as the empty string instead, so the same KB produced different hashes for a missing value depending on which path recorded it.

- A write could report success and persist nothing when the SDK exposed a read-only `Dirty`. `WriteService`'s force-dirty step called `SetValue` with no `CanWrite` and no type check; on a major where `Dirty` is read-only the call threw, the enclosing catch swallowed it, and both the part and its owning object stayed clean. The same unguarded shape was in `VariableInjector` with a partial `CanWrite` check. All four dirty-marking sites now go through one helper that tries both the `Dirty` and `IsDirty` aliases, requires a writable bool, and reports false instead of throwing — so a part that could not be marked is visible rather than silent.

- A `Try`-prefixed probe could throw. `KbStartupService.TryInvokeGetPropertyValue` had no try/catch at all, so a throwing `GetPropertyValue` escaped a method whose name promised it would not, leaving the caller's guard as the only thing between a bad property read and a failed KB startup. Its `SetPropertyValue` sibling also matched only the first of two parameters, so on a major declaring `SetPropertyValue(string, int)` it could bind the wrong overload and fail at invoke time with a cast error. Both are gone: the getter never throws, and the setter checks both parameter types.

- A `FindMethod ?? FindNonPublicMethod` fallback in `WebFormSaveDiagnostics` could never fire — the two helpers were byte-identical. The dead fallback read as defensive coverage that did not exist, so a member that was absent looked like one that had been looked for two ways.

### Internal

- The SDK-probe helpers were consolidated onto one `Helpers.SdkReflection`, and the copies were not interchangeable. Roughly 90 private helper bodies were near-duplicated across services: five distinct names (`SafeStr`, `SafeString`, `SafeGet`, `SafeReadString`, `Safe`) for the same three-line function, six base-chain method finders, nine property readers that disagreed on whether they guarded indexer properties and whether they searched case-insensitively, and six `Dirty` setters with four different guard combinations. They differed in visibility flags, in indexer handling, and in what a null input meant — so a member could resolve on one path and be missed on another for reasons no caller could see. The canonical helper documents the conventions (never throws; walks the base chain; public and non-public; rejects indexers; a `Try` prefix means it) so a future copy knows what it is duplicating.

- `Truncate` is now one implementation, and the copies disagreed about arithmetic. Six of the eight appended the ellipsis after slicing to `max`, returning `max + 1` characters; two sliced to `max - 1` and returned `max`. Any caller sizing a buffer, computing a budget, or comparing lengths got a different answer depending on which service produced the string. The result is now bounded by the requested width.

- `KbEntityIdentity` replaces three byte-identical private helper triples in `IndexCacheService`, `KbService` and `ListService`, and reads `Key` once instead of three times. Identical copies are still three places for a change to miss: an entry gaining a fourth key would have been added to one and not the others, and the index would carry it only for objects first seen through that path.

- The theme class catalog is its own `genexus_analyze` mode, and the typed-create experiment no longer runs on every WebForm write. Both came out of a code-quality review of this change set.

  `genexus_analyze mode=theme_classes` lists the KB's `ThemeClass` objects so an agent can pick a class by name instead of guessing (roadmap W6). Live on GX18/KBTeste it returns all 129, sorted by name, with `truncated` marking a cut. It was first shipped as `genexus_layout action=list_controls includeThemeClasses=true`, which was the wrong home: the control list comes from `IUserControlsManagerService` and the catalog from the search index, so one action answered two unrelated questions behind a boolean gate. The control list is back to a single response shape. Two measured findings are reported rather than papered over. The loaded index is the only complete enumeration available headlessly — a `KBModel.Objects.GetAll()` walk and a theme's own style tree each surface a single theme-root object, so neither is a fallback; and the discriminator is the index entry `Type`, not the `TypeIndex` bucket, whose storage-key format is internal to `IndexCacheService` (a key-based read resolved to 1 entry where the KB has 129). `objectGuid` is the class KB object's GUID and is **not** the value a layout `class` attribute takes: a measured layout attribute (`<guid>-<suffix>`) matched none of the 129, so the response carries `authorableInLayoutClassAttribute: false` and authoring a class stays on the layout-document path. An index with no `ThemeClass` entries answers `ThemeClassesUnavailable` with a reason rather than a partial list, and `controlTypes` is reported unavailable instead of guessed.

  The blocked `WebFormTypedCreateRouter` attempt is now behind `ApplyEditableXml`'s `attemptTypedCreate` flag, **off by default**. On every measured GeneXus 18 major `Create` binds but `SaveProperties` returns false and the control is stripped, so running it cost an SDK round-trip per created tag and changed nothing — it could not make a write worse and could not make one better. The flag keeps the experiment reproducible on demand instead of charging every structural write for it. The three live findings the attempt encodes (the node handed to `Create` must live in the part document, an element needs an assigned `id` to be addressable by the SDK's tag enumeration, `SaveProperties` returns false headless) are preserved in the code and the roadmap.

  The two typed WebForm writers no longer carry parallel reflection helpers. `WebFormTypedPropertyWriter` and `WebFormTypedCreateRouter` each had their own type lookup, property read, part-document resolution, element lookup and tag index, and the copies had already drifted: one `FindElementInPartDoc` matched `id | ControlName | controlName | InternalName | name` while the other matched only three of those, and one tag index preferred the `(KBObject, XmlDocument)` `EnumerateWebTag` overload while the other only looked for the single-argument form — so a control could resolve on the property path and miss on the create path for reasons no test could explain. One `WebFormSdkReflection` now owns all of it, plus the third inline enumeration that had accumulated in the same file. `WebFormStructuralChange.ChangeType` became the `WebFormStructuralChangeKind` enum (its predicate was written as a string literal in three files), and the detector's child pairing became a named `ChildPairing` instead of a `bool[]` plus a `List<Tuple<int,int>>` re-walked three times — one of those walks iterated in match order while another sorted, which is where the move report could have been wrong.

  A preview that lands on an auth wall now says which wall it is and what to do about it. `genexus_browser action=preview` answered a GAM-redirected target with a bare `status: auth_required` and, at most, a sentence saying injection was attempted — three different recoveries behind one shape, so an agent had to string-match the message to learn whether to pass credentials, fix them, or authenticate out of band. The response now carries a `code` naming the recovery (`GamLoginRequired`, `GamLoginRejected`, `AuthRequired`) plus `authWall`, `credentialsSupplied`, `finalUrl`, a `hint` and a `nextStep`, and no credential is echoed back. `GamLoginRejected` is deliberately distinct from `GamLoginRequired`: retrying with the same refused credentials cannot succeed, so its hint points at the account rather than at the parm. The worker-serialization half of the recorded item is unchanged and still tracked — the per-step timeout and wall-clock budget bound the call, but a slow target still holds the SDK thread. Pinned by six `PreviewAuthWallTests`, mutation-checked by breaking the GAM-URL classification and confirming the guard goes red.

- `genexus_layout action=list_controls` gains an opt-in theme class catalog (`includeThemeClasses=true`), closing the first step of roadmap W6. An agent authoring a layout previously had to guess a class name, and nothing in the tool surface listed the classes a KB actually defines. Live on GX18/KBTeste the catalog returns all 129 `ThemeClass` objects, sorted by name, with `truncated` marking a cut. Two measured findings shaped the design and are reported rather than papered over. (1) The loaded index is the only complete enumeration: a direct `KBModel.Objects.GetAll()` walk and a theme's own style tree each surface a single theme-root object where the index holds 129, so neither is used as a fallback - serving that one entry would look like a complete answer. The discriminator is the index entry `Type`, not the `TypeIndex` bucket, whose storage-key format is internal to `IndexCacheService`; a key-based read resolved to 1 entry where the KB has 129. (2) `objectGuid` is the class KB object's GUID and is **not** the value a layout `class` attribute takes: a measured layout attribute (`<guid>-<suffix>`) matches none of the KB's class GUIDs, so the response carries `authorableInLayoutClassAttribute: false` and states that authoring `class` stays on the layout-document path. An index with no `ThemeClass` entries answers `ThemeClassesUnavailable` with a reason rather than a partial list, and `controlTypes` is reported unavailable instead of being guessed. Covered by eleven new `ThemeClassCatalogTests` (including one that fails if the catalog regresses to reading the candidate bucket).

- `WebFormPropertyDeltaDetector` now classifies whole-element layout changes instead of rejecting them opaquely. An add/remove/move of controls (matched by element name + control identity, order-sensitive) is reported as `StructuralChanges[]` with a `structural: …` reason naming each change (`added <gxButton> 'Btn' under /GxMultiForm/Form/body`), while `IsSupported` stays false and attribute `Deltas` stay empty until the typed writer gains a Create/Remove path (roadmap W1 step 1). The previous `child count differs` / `control identity differs` reasons named only the divergence point. Covered by seven new `WebFormPropertyDeltaTests`; existing attribute-only behavior is unchanged. Follow-up refinement from live evidence: pairing no longer keys on element `id` (the SDK regenerates ids on subtree materialization, which produced phantom removed+added pairs after every full-tree write) and `id` is excluded from attribute deltas as SDK-managed; delta keys stay id-first for the writer's by-id resolution. Nested adds record the whole subtree, and the gate attempts whenever at least one identified control is added with no removals/moves. Roadmap status: steps 1-3 landed; the acceptance criterion (a `gxButton OnClickEvent` reaching the browser as the right `data-gx-evt`) is still open, and control removal via the SDK is still unverified.

- New `Helpers.WebFormTypedCreateRouter` wires the attempt into `LayoutFix` (roadmap W1 step 3). When the gate passes, each added control is materialized through the probed `WebTagFactory.Create` (parent tag resolved from the live enumeration, `null` when the parent is itself new) and the raw rewrite always runs afterwards with the existing post-write verification as the fail-closed gate. Live on GX18/KBTeste: `Typed-create materialized <gxTextBlock>` binds and returns, but the control is still stripped on push - binding is proven, attach-into-stored-model is the remaining step. Pure helpers (`ShouldAttemptTypedCreate`, `IsControlAdd`, depth ordering, `ImportInto`, `ReassertCreatedTags`, element/parent resolution, factory-type resolution) covered by twenty-eight `WebFormTypedCreateRouterTests`; the SDK-touching attempt is live-covered. Three live findings are recorded rather than papered over: the node handed to `Create` must live in the PART document (a node from a detached editable document is invisible to the part), an element without an `id` is unaddressable by the SDK's tag enumeration so an id is assigned on import, and even with both fixes `SaveProperties` returns false on GeneXus 18 headless - the created control is not persisted and the existing post-write verification reports the difference. Persisting an added control is blocked on that major; the attempt stays as fail-closed instrumentation and the raw-rewrite behaviour is unchanged.

- New `Compatibility.WebTagFactoryAdapter` probes the SDK's control-creation member structurally (roadmap W1 step 2). GeneXus 18 exposes static `WebTagFactory.Create(XmlNode, KBObject, IWebTag, bool)` (verified by direct reflection against the installed SDK, which also shows `IWebTag` carries no Remove/Delete member - removal stays on the document/`EditableToStored` path); other majors may reshape or drop it. The adapter takes the factory `Type`, scores `Create` overloads by argument compatibility (trailing `bool` required, equally-compatible overloads fail closed like `AmbiguousMatchException`), reports absent members as false, and propagates SDK rejections instead of degrading into false "unsupported". Covered by nine `WebTagFactoryAdapterTests` over fake shapes; the real GX18 shape is proven by the reflection dump, not by a test that would couple the suite to one major.

- Two new PowerShell regression guards, both registered in the release script suite. `test-release-changelog.ps1` promotes a `CHANGELOG.md` fixture and asserts the body moves to the version heading, the fresh `## Unreleased` carries the anchor, the anchor is not substantive, a real bullet is, a repeated promotion is a no-op, a later promotion never rewrites a published section, and a changelog with no `## Unreleased` still fails loudly. `test-collect-diagnostics.ps1` runs the real collector against a fixture `worker_debug.log` and fails if any token, bearer payload, connection-string component, phrase secret, quoted value, literal npm/GitHub prefix, user name or host name reaches the bundle - while the log markers, the redaction markers and the KB-path redaction must still be there. The existing contract, publication and integration-preflight tests gained the redaction and binding-diagnostic coverage for the three issues above.

## v3.9.3 - 2026-09-28


### Tracked issues

- [#325](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/325) — [Bug] history_get pode exceder o prazo do cliente sem permitir acompanhar o resultado


### Fixed

- A long `genexus_versioning` read could outlast the client's window and leave the caller with no documented way to reach the result the Gateway had already stored ([#325](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/325)). A read-only `history_get` was measured in the field at 352 s of wall clock, of which 345 s was SDK time, while the caller gave up at 60 s. Every signal needed to recover pointed the wrong way: the operationId existed only in the reply that never arrived, so the only way to obtain it was to read gateway telemetry; and the record itself, once the worker finally answered, read as a contradiction - `timedOut: true` beside `status: Completed` and a populated `workerPayload` - which looks like a failure rather than a result waiting to be read. The mutation tools already answered this shape of problem with an accept-then-poll envelope; the version-store reads had no equivalent, which is the actual gap. `history_get`, `history_list`, `diff`, `diff_generated` and `blame` now accept `async: true` and return `{ operationId, job_id, pollTarget: 'op:<id>', status: 'running' }` BEFORE the SDK call starts, running the read detached and storing its envelope, so the result stays fetchable through `genexus_lifecycle action=status/result` (and cancellable through `action=cancel`) no matter when the original turn ends. The KB-mutating actions (`history_save`, `history_restore`, `undo`, `time_travel`) are deliberately excluded: they keep the mutation path, whose recovery fences assume a write may already have persisted. Separately, the three signals a caller conflated are now published as three fields on every timed-out operation - `timedOut` (the wait expired), `status` (the worker's own outcome) and the new `resultAvailable` (a payload is stored to read) - with a hint that names the retrieval call, so a terminal record carrying a result reads as a result instead of a contradiction. Untimed operations are unchanged: no `resultAvailable`, no hint.

- Every release now completes its final publication check, and issues labeled `fixed-pending-release` are closed automatically again. The release script builds its status as an ordered dictionary, but the shared contract probed for a key with `PSObject.Properties['name']`, which never sees a dictionary's keys — every summary, snapshot, and evidence file arrives from `ConvertFrom-Json` as an object whose keys *do* surface there, so the shape difference stayed invisible until the one caller that used a dictionary. The resulting guard was permanently false, so a published release was still reported as `commits do not match` even when the tag, assets, workflow, and npm publication had all verified. That check is the gate immediately before the label-driven issue closure, so no release ever reached the close step and labeled issues stayed open indefinitely. A single dual-shape presence helper now backs all fifteen probes in the module. The same blind spot also let an ordered-dictionary status skip the repository consistency check entirely, so a status naming the wrong repository was accepted; it is now rejected.

## v3.9.2 - 2026-09-27


### Tracked issues

- [#321](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/321) — [Docs] `GXMCP_BUSY_REJECT_MS` exige reinício do Worker, e o knob `busyWaitMs` por comando segue promovido sem encaminhamento
- [#322](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/322) — [Bug][Follow-up #317] `test-release-doctor` falha com `$env:TEMP` em forma 8.3
- [#323](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/323) — [Bug] `release-preflight` não persiste a saída das fases: summary de uma fase vermelha fica sem evidência
- [#324](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/324) — [Bug] `nexus-ide`: teste de inline completion que grava configuração Global excede o timeout de 2000 ms (1 ocorrência; causa não isolada)

### Changed

- The Worker's response path now serializes **once** instead of twice, and parses once instead of twice. `SendResponse` previously ran `JsonConvert.SerializeObject` over the whole envelope twice on every call — the second pass existed only to embed the measured `serializeMs`/`responseBytes` into `_meta.telemetry`. The measured values are only known *after* serialization, so they now go in through a GUID-scoped placeholder that is replaced by a single substring splice of the small telemetry object; the splice is anchored on the `"telemetry"` key and falls back to the untouched frame if the anchor is absent, so a payload can never be corrupted by the optimization. Separately, `McpResponseNormalizer.Normalize` parsed the entire payload, discarded the tree on the success path and returned the original string, which `SendResponse` then re-parsed with `JsonIngress.ParseToken` — two full parse passes over identical text. `NormalizeToken` now hands the token forward, and the dispatcher surfaces it so the envelope is framed directly from it. Callers without a token (idempotency replay) keep the old parse, and `Normalize`'s string contract is unchanged: a payload needing no rewrite is still returned verbatim, so an idempotency replay stays byte-identical. Measured with `ResponseEnvelopeSerializationBenchmark` (BenchmarkDotNet, ShortRun, in-process; synthetic envelopes at 1, 400 and 20 000 result rows, both pipelines fed the identical dispatch string): small envelope **6.43 µs → 3.50 µs (-45%)**, 26.85 KB → 17.85 KB allocated (-34%); large envelope **111.5 ms → 50.1 ms (-55%)**, 126.8 MB → 81.6 MB allocated (-36%). The 400-row case measured 1082.7 µs → 872.2 µs but its confidence interval spans ±98% of the mean, so it is reported as unconfirmed rather than as a -19% result. The win scales with payload size, which is why the large-envelope figure is the one that matters for `genexus_read` and bulk exports; the large case here is a synthetic far bigger than a typical read, so treat the ratio as the signal and the absolute figures as an upper bound. One deliberate telemetry consequence: the response parse used to be charged to `_meta.telemetry.transformMs` (measured inside `SendResponse`) and now happens inside the dispatcher, so it falls inside the `sdkMs` window instead. `transformMs` therefore reports ~0 for a normal response and `avgSdkMs` absorbs a small parse cost that is not SDK time. The attribution is more truthful in the sense that the work is dispatch work rather than response framing, but `avgSdkMs` should no longer be read as pure GeneXus SDK cost; the dispatch/SDK split would need a separate phase measurement to recover, which this change does not attempt.
- Corrected the `docs/environment_variables.md` STA scheduler section from [#319](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/319): both variables are read from the **Worker process environment**, so neither takes effect until a new Worker starts from a process that already carries the new value, and the per-mode restart procedure (`isolated`, `shared-host` broker lifetime, legacy shared Gateway) is now spelled out. The `WorkerBusy` wait hint no longer advertises a per-command `busyWaitMs` override, which no Gateway conversion path forwards: routing either nests tool arguments one level below where the Worker reads the knob, or maps a closed field list, and the client `_meta` is rebuilt by the Gateway. The published per-command override is a separate runtime change; the doc states plainly that the environment variable is the only reachable control today. This is an erratum only, and it also covers the `busyWaitMs` mention in the [#293](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/293) (v3.9.0) entry. No functional change; the only altered MCP output is the `WorkerBusy` hint text. ([#321](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/321))
- `genexus_module` dry-run previews for `install` by name and `install_builtin` now report real packaged dependencies. A name-based preview previously returned no dependency information at all, so a caller could not distinguish "this module needs nothing" from "the SDK was never asked". `dependencySource` makes that explicit with three states: `modulePart` (read from the module's own `ModuleContentPart.ExportDependencies`, the same part the install flow already trusts for its dependency comparison, and measured present on GeneXus 16, 17 and 18), `sdk` (fell back to `IModuleManagerService.GetReferencesFromPackagableObjects`, GeneXus 18 only), and `unavailable` (nothing could be read, so the empty list is not authoritative). Each entry carries `name`/`version`/`minimumVersion`/`maximumVersion`/`id`/`expose` plus `installedInKb`. The read is a pure enrichment of a read-only preview: it is guarded, cannot throw, and an unreadable shape yields `unavailable` rather than a misleading empty list. This also corrects the source order used in the first pass of this work: reading the GeneXus 18-only service member first reported `unavailable` on GeneXus 16 and 17 even though a cross-major source was already in use elsewhere in the same flow.
- SDK member probes now go through a single `Compatibility.SdkMemberProbe` boundary instead of hand-written `BindingFlags` at ~24 call sites. The load-bearing part is `FlattenHierarchy`, whose effect is **not** uniform and which is therefore easy to get wrong by assumption: measured on .NET Framework 4.8, an *instance* member declared on a base class is already found without it because instance lookup walks the hierarchy anyway, while a *static* member on a base class is found **only** with it. A static helper that a major hoists up its hierarchy would previously have been reported as a missing capability — a silent, fail-closed capability loss. `DeclaredOnly` sites are deliberately untouched, since that flag means the opposite of what a compatibility probe wants, and `AmbiguousMatchException` is now caught and reported as *absent* so a doubly-declared member degrades the capability instead of binding a base class arbitrarily.

### Fixed

- `genexus_worker_reload mode=hard` failed on **every** call, and un-cleanly. `CopyWorkerBinaries` required both `GxMcp.Worker.exe` and `GxMcp.Worker.dll`, but the Worker is a net48 project with `OutputType=Exe` — the exe *is* the assembly, and no `GxMcp.Worker.dll` is produced in `bin\Debug`, `bin\Release`, `publish\worker` or the staged runtime. The guard came from a change that converted an earlier best-effort skip into a hard failure and named a file that cannot exist. Failing loudly was the right call; naming an impossible file made the tool unusable. The required set is now `GxMcp.Worker.exe` plus `GxMcp.Worker.exe.config` (binding redirects and SDK probing — a swapped worker will not resolve the GeneXus SDK without it), with `.pdb` and `.dll` copied when present. A structural test now derives the expectation from the csproj's `OutputType` and validates the *real* `publish\worker` and `bin\Debug` directories, which is precisely what the old guard rejected.
- The same reload also left the KB with **no worker at all** whenever the copy failed. The swap runs inside `DrainAndReplaceAsync`'s post-drain hook, so the old worker had already been stopped, its exit confirmed and `entry.Worker` cleared before the throw. Two independent causes are fixed. (a) An unusable `sourceDir` is now validated **before** the drain and answered with a structured `WorkerSwapSourceInvalid` error, so a typo'd path no longer costs a live worker. (b) A `sourceDir` equal to the running worker's own directory is a no-op instead of a copy: it previously copied `GxMcp.Worker.exe` onto itself, which throws a sharing violation while the outgoing worker still holds the exe — observed live as `The process cannot access the file ... GxMcp.Worker.exe because it is being used by another process`, failing the reload *after* the drain. The pool's `finally` already recovered the entry correctly (no wedge), which is why this presented as "reload doesn't work" rather than as a crash.
- The automatic index bootstrap never ran on the modern Streamable-HTTP transport, so an HTTP client could sit against a healthy Worker and an unindexed KB indefinitely. The bootstrap ended with `await WorkerWarmupCompleted.Task` carrying no timeout and no cancellation, and that task is only ever completed by `TriggerWorkerWarmupOnce`'s `finally`. The warm pass is started from `initialize` **only** when `sessionContextEnabled` is true, and `Program.Http.cs` passes `sessionContextEnabled: !modern` — so a modern client never started it. The bootstrap had already consumed its one-shot per-alias flag by then, which is why the later `OnWorkerStarted` and `genexus_kb action=open` triggers could not retry it: the bootstrap parked on the await forever and `BulkIndex` was never sent. The read gate could not heal this either, because `Cold` is deliberately excluded from `IsRestoredSnapshotAwaitingDelta` and the one tool that would have started a build from inside the Worker (`genexus_query`) is itself index-gated, leaving a manual `genexus_lifecycle action=index` as the only escape. The wait is now conditional and bounded: it is skipped outright when the warm pass was never started on this transport, and otherwise gives up after `WarmupBeforeBootstrapWaitCeilingMs` (20 s) and proceeds. The serialization the wait existed to guarantee is preserved — a running warm pass is still awaited — because a wedged warm pass must not be able to hold the index bootstrap hostage, since the bootstrap is what starts the index at all.
- `whoami` could report a fabricated `index.status: "Cold"` and then advise a full forced reindex of a KB that was already indexed. When the 400 ms index-state round-trip times out right after a KB open, whoami stamps a placeholder carrying `Status="Cold"`/`TotalObjects=0` with `RefreshedAtUtc=UtcNow`. The timestamp is deliberate and load-bearing — it makes the snapshot look fresh, which suppresses further round-trips against a busy worker for the whole 15 s window — but the `Cold` was a guess, indistinguishable from an observed one, and `suggestedNext` routed it to `genexus_lifecycle action=index force=true`. Observed live against a real KB: the first whoami reported `Cold`/0 objects and advised a forced reindex, and the next second the worker reported `Ready`/`freshness=current` with 620 objects, `canDelta: true`, `slotGeneration: 145` — the advised reindex was pure waste, and on a large KB it is a multi-minute rebuild. The optimization is kept and the lie is removed: the placeholder is now built by one named, testable factory and marked `Provisional`, `index.provisional` is published so a client can tell an invented state from an observed one, and advice routes on **provenance before content** — a provisional snapshot asks for a re-read instead of proposing a mutating reindex, while a worker-observed `Cold` keeps its `force=true` nudge. `IsIndexUsableForReads` still fails closed on a provisional snapshot, so the read gate is unchanged; only the advice is.
- `genexus_query` could serve a pre-edit result from its response cache. `SearchService`'s 512-entry LRU has no TTL and was dropped only when `index.LastUpdated` advanced, but a write never advances `LastUpdated`: the write path (`genexus_edit`, `genexus_io`, pattern apply) goes through `IndexCacheService.UpdateEntry`, which calls `TouchGraph` and bumps `GraphRevision` via `Interlocked.Increment`, while `LastUpdated` moves only on a bulk walk (`AddOrUpdateBatch`/`ReplaceAll`). A query issued before an edit therefore kept answering with the pre-edit answer until its LRU slot happened to be evicted. `ListService` already keyed its own cache on both counters; `SearchService` now does the same, and the rule is extracted into a testable `InvalidateQueryCacheIfStale`. The common path was partly masked by the Gateway's per-KB generation invalidation, but a differing-argument query or an evicted Gateway entry could reach the stale Worker-level copy. The comparison is inequality, not `>`, so a rebuilt index restarting `GraphRevision` at 1 is also detected.
- `Test-GxMcpReleasePreflightCompatibility` compared the recorded and expected `root`, `gxPath`, `liveKbPath` and `liveFixtureManifest` by raw string equality, so a preflight summary and freshly derived inputs that named the same directory differently (8.3 short name, dot segments, trailing separator) were rejected and the operator lost a reusable artifact for reasons unrelated to repository state. Both sides are now normalized through one helper before comparison, and the process-smoke temp prefix check normalizes both of its sides through the same helper instead of mixing `GetFullPath` with an unnormalized temp root. Rooted paths only: a relative value has no stable base and stays a strict comparison, and a genuinely different root still invalidates reuse. `test-release-contract` pins the equivalent forms and the genuinely-different case. ([#322](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/322))
- `test-release-doctor` recorded a fixture `root` built from the raw `$env:TEMP`, while the doctor derives its expected root with `[IO.Path]::GetFullPath`. On a host whose `%TEMP%` is an 8.3 short path the two were different strings, the certificate was refused, and the matching-preflight case threw `Doctor did not recommend the safe artifact reuse for a matching preflight`. The fixture now records the root the way the real preflight does. Verified by running the suite with `TEMP=C:\Projetos\GENEXU~1`: it fails against the pre-fix code and passes after. ([#322](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/322))
- `release-preflight.ps1` printed a tail of each phase's output and then discarded it, so the summary it persists — the artifact that survives the terminal — carried no output at all. `status` and `exitCode` were correct, so the gate never changed its verdict, but answering "what broke?" after the fact meant rerunning the phase, and the console evidence it did print was ambiguous: `Invoke-PreflightParallel` starts all six phases before completing any, so every header printed before every tail, with no phase label. Every phase that actually started a process now records `stdoutPath` and `stderrPath` pointing at `<summary>.<phase-slug>.{stdout,stderr}.log` next to the summary, which follows the `integration-preflight.ps1` precedent: the phase carries the path and the content stays in the file. Both files are written even when empty, because an empty log is evidence while a missing one is indistinguishable from a phase that never ran. A phase that never ran (skipped, dry-run, reused, executable absent) carries `null`. The three `Write-Error` sites now cite the log paths; the final one previously pointed only at the summary that held no evidence. `unavailable` — the one terminal status that ended with `reason = null` — now records its reason. `Protect-GxMcpReleaseMessage` was split into a new uncapped `Protect-GxMcpReleaseText` holding the union of all three credential-pattern sets the repository applies (the contract's literal `ghp_`/`github_pat_`/`npm_` prefixes and key/value pairs, plus integration-preflight's `user id`/`userid` words) and the existing 1200-character summary cap layered on top, so a persisted log cannot leak a secret the console tail would have masked while doctor and status reports stay bounded. Writes happen in the same `finally` as before, and `$State.Stdout`/`$State.Stderr` stay raw so the host-blocker classifier still sees the original text — the guarantee is ordering, not a promise. ([#323](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/323))
- `release-preflight: parallel` harness cleanup also stopped leaking. Its cleanup was gated on the summary still existing, which is exactly the moment the derived log pair is orphaned, and the ad-hoc summary paths it reassigns `$SummaryPath` to were never removed at all. Both are fixed: logs are removed first and unconditionally, and every summary path the test assigns is tracked and cleaned. ([#323](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/323))
- The `Nexus IDE checks` phase no longer depends on a clean extension-host profile to pass. `GxInlineCompletionItemProvider` tests are the only ones in the suite that write real configuration, and they target the `Global` store, which is a disk-backed `settings.json` under the (cwd-derived) `.vscode-test/user-data-dir`. Because a mocha timeout does not cancel a pending async function, a write of `true` landing after a timeout could pair its restore out of order or skip it entirely when the host exits first, leaving `inlineCompletion.ai = true` persisted and failing the "disabled" test on every later run. Each test now writes the value it needs before asserting, the `try` wraps the write so the restore is always paired with the same invocation, and `suiteSetup`/`teardown` neutralize whatever a previous or leaked run left behind, so the suite is self-healing rather than order- and history-dependent. The declared default is now asserted from the package contribution as a pure read, which keeps the "defaults to off" claim honest without touching the profile, and the suite carries a measured 10 s timeout (the two configuration tests measured 46-426 ms isolated, 184 ms under the preflight parallel wave) scoped to this suite, leaving the runner's global 2000 ms default and the other 109 tests untouched. The reported single occurrence did not reproduce here under a concurrent `dotnet test` wave, so the timeout itself remains a mitigation rather than a root-cause fix; the state-corruption hazard it created is fixed. ([#324](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/324))
- The GeneXus 18 compatibility guards for `genexus_module install_builtin` could not be tested at all. `ModuleBuiltinCheckUnsupported` and `ModuleBuiltinVersionUnsupported` appeared only at their own throw sites and in the changelog: zero tests, zero documentation, zero inventory entries, and `install_builtin` was exercised by nothing at all. That is the entire behavioral payload of the v3.9.1 GX18 compatibility fix. The reason it was untestable is structural, and the fix is a seam: each probe now takes the type to probe as a parameter (`TryIsBuiltInModuleOn` / `TryGetBuiltinModuleVersionOn`), because on a GeneXus 18 build every real `IModuleManagerService` implementation necessarily carries the member, so the "member absent" branch was unreachable from any test compiled against the primary SDK. A regression that made a failed probe return success would have installed an unverified built-in module while the whole suite stayed green. Measured against this repo's installs, `IModuleManagerService` exposes 17 members on GeneXus 16, 23 on 17 and 28 on 18; `IsBuiltInModule` is 17+, `GetBuiltinModuleVersion` is 18-only, `GetSettings` is 17+. The new tests pin the present, absent, wrong-signature, throwing and null cases, and the degradation codes are documented in `docs/agent_playbook.md` with the per-major meaning spelled out — an unverifiable module is refused, never installed.
- `SdkDeletionAdapter` — the adapter that exists precisely to absorb the `Delete()` versus `Remove()` difference between SDK majors — had one call site and no tests, so all four of its branches were uncovered. It now has tests for preferring `Delete`, falling back to `Remove` on a legacy major, reporting absent when neither exists, and propagating an SDK rejection rather than swallowing it into a false "unsupported".
- The `genexus_module` tool-help entry now documents the `dependencySource` contract and the per-major built-in refusal codes. This is the reachable place, not `tool_definitions.json`: `ToolProfileFilter.Filter` compacts *every* profile including `all` and replaces each `tools/list` description with a `genexus://kb/tool-help/<name>` pointer, so a tool's schema description is never served verbatim to any client. The schema description was extended as well, but only the help entry is what a caller actually reads.

## v3.9.1 - 2026-09-25


### Tracked issues

- [#304](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/304) — [Bug][Follow-up #294] Runtime GC can partially delete a runtime still used by a live Gateway/broker/Worker
- [#305](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/305) — [Follow-up #291] Lifecycle operations: queue instead of BuildAlreadyRunning, delta status, unified operation ids, remaining long actions
- [#306](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/306) — [Follow-up #293] STA scheduler: client identity never reaches the Worker in shared-host (round-robin inert); scans not sliced
- [#307](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/307) — [Follow-up #292] Source store is only filled lazily: add background backfill and warm-start refresh
- [#308](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/308) — [Bug][Data loss] Any WebForm write on a GeneXus 17 legacy HTML WebForm strips every `CaptionExpression` (all labels, tab captions and button texts disappear)
- [#309](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/309) — [Bug][Safety] Visual (WebForm) writes commit before verifying: `rollbackOnFailure` is ignored, the response says `persisted: false` although the change is saved, and `dryRun`/`validate:"only"` cannot predict the damage
- [#310](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/310) — [Bug] `genexus_edit_form add_button` / `add_textblock` produce markup that does not match GX17 legacy HTML WebForms, and the schema hints warn about valid legacy attributes
- [#311](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/311) — [Bug] `PostTimeoutReadRequired` fence after a timed-out `genexus_variable` cannot be cleared by any read the hint suggests (fence is keyed to part `Source`; whole-object reads never qualify)
- [#312](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/312) — [Regression 3.9.0] `genexus_search_source` ignores `scope` (`webForm`, `rules`, `conditions`, `layout`) once the source store holds the object's primary part
- [#313](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/313) — [Feature][Follow-up #260] K2BTools WebPanelDesigner objects (layout form, no PatternInstance) are invisible to the pattern tools: support reading/editing their grid definition and regenerating editor methods
- [#314](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/314) — [Feature] Procedure report layouts: add, move and remove controls inside print blocks (today only print blocks themselves can be managed)
- [#315](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/315) — [Feature] `genexus_variable`: support dimensioned (array) variables, and expose dimensions on read
- [#316](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/316) — [DX] Papercuts seen on 3.9.0: `inspect include=variables` empty for a K2B WebPanel, `read part=WebForm` ignores `offset/limit`, `lifecycle status wait` on `op:<id>` returns immediately
- [#318](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/318) — [DX] `Nexus IDE checks` falha sem causa classificada nem remediação quando há update do VS Code em andamento no host (mutex `vscode-updating`)
- [#319](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/319) — [Docs] `GXMCP_BUSY_WAIT_MS` e `GXMCP_BUSY_REJECT_MS` sem entrada em `docs/environment_variables.md`


### Changed

- Documented `GXMCP_BUSY_WAIT_MS` and `GXMCP_BUSY_REJECT_MS` in `docs/environment_variables.md`, including the STA queue semantics, the per-command override precedence, and that both are read per request. ([#319](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/319))
- Expanded the published tool contract for the typed WWP grid, lifecycle continuation, semantic WebForm safety, report-control, K2B/structural variable, and pagination work. The all-profile schema budget is now 33,500 tokens / 82,000 bytes (measured ~33,055 tokens / 81,233 bytes); generated discovery, capabilities, and operation-contract artifacts are regenerated from the source-of-truth schemas. ([#268](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/268), [#306](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/306), [#308](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/308), [#309](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/309), [#310](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/310), [#313](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/313), [#314](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/314), [#315](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/315), [#316](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/316))

### Fixed

- `genexus_module` no longer calls GeneXus 18-only `IModuleManagerService` members directly. `GetBuiltinModuleVersion` (GeneXus 18 only) and `GetSettings` (GeneXus 17+) are now resolved by guarded reflection, so the Worker compiles against every supported major. The built-in route reports `ModuleBuiltinCheckUnsupported` or `ModuleBuiltinVersionUnsupported` when the installed SDK cannot verify the registration or the exact version, and a missing module cache directory still refuses the install with `ModuleCacheUnavailable` rather than skipping the cache-identity check this flow promises. Caught by the pre-release live matrix, where GeneXus 17 failed to compile; a primary-SDK-only build and test run cannot see it.
- `scripts/test-live-matrix.ps1` now honours its documented default. An omitted `-Majors` binds to `$null`, and `@($null).Count` is 1 rather than 0, so the "select every catalog major" branch was never taken and the run died with a parameter-binding error before reporting a single major. Blank entries are treated as omitted, an unresolvable selection is refused explicitly, and `Write-MatrixSummary` accepts an empty result set so a build that fails before any major runs can still be reported instead of crashing the failure path itself. The existing test passed `@()`, which is not what an omitted flag produces.
- Local module installation now validates package metadata and SDK staging inputs before calling the native installer, addressing the uncontextualized null-argument failure reported in [#274](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/274). `install` and `install_builtin` support readonly dependency/object/version plans, conflict checks, idempotent inventory readback and truthful partial-persistence receipts without implicit lifecycle or unverified rollback. Timeout results prohibit automatic retry; live reproduction of the reported operational cause remains a separate validation. No discovery budget increase. ([#275](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/pull/275))
- `move_grid_column` no longer half-applies a rejected request: the caption is written only after the grid, the column identity and the before-anchor have all resolved, so a refused move leaves the PatternInstance byte-identical. `add_grid_variable` now also refuses a `variableReference` that is already bound to another column, instead of only rejecting a duplicate name. The `gridPath` selector is resolved as an absolute type path walked from the document root with optional zero-based indices (strict shape, typed `InvalidGridPath`/`GridNotFound`/`AmbiguousGrid`); the previous resolver matched segments against the grid element itself, so every absolute path failed and only the single-grid-without-path form worked. 43 focused tests cover the mutation plan, adapted from the cases proposed in PR #269 and re-anchored on the stricter argument contract this server enforces. ([#269](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/pull/269), [#268](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/268))
- Native identity-based builds now consume the SDK's equivalent result-bearing `Build` overload with the same object keys and zero options instead of interpreting a non-throwing `void BuildWithTheseOnly` call as success. Missing result support is reported as `NativeBuildOutcomeUnknown`; native failure is reported as `NativeBuildFailed`, without automatic per-target retries. In-process builds no longer invent an MSBuild process exit code. Output-root verification uses the opened KB and the active environment's target model/property bag, and reports a typed error when unavailable. Detailed SDK compiler-output capture remains unavailable on this native route; the SDK result is not an itemized compiler log. ([#282](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/pull/282))
- Fresh verification reads now evict retained SDK object instances after invalidation before resolving through the public read route. This addresses `FreshReadUnavailable` in DesignSystem Tokens/Styles full and patch previews without relaxing GUID/reference checks, destination pins, concurrency tokens or exact-source verification. An opt-in read-only live regression checks both previews leave source and version tokens unchanged. ([#282](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/pull/282))
- The `Nexus IDE checks` preflight phase now classifies a VS Code updater block from its own captured output and records the cause, an actionable remediation, and the mutex confirmation on the phase (`details.code = 'vscode-update-in-progress'`). The phase deliberately stays `failed`: the aggregate accepts `unavailable` as an approved terminal status, so downgrading it would certify a release that ran no Electron test. The confirmation probe is Windows-only and resolves the mutex from the same runtime the test launches, following `runTest.ts` version resolution and the top-level `win32MutexName`; when it cannot resolve, it reports unconfirmed instead of guessing. ([#318](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/318))
- `test-release-preflight.ps1` no longer reports a false negative when `%TEMP%` is an 8.3 short path: the recorded live KB path and the fixture path are compared through the same release path normalization instead of raw string equality. ([#317](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/317))
- The resume/certificate case in `test-release-preflight.ps1` is now hermetic. It previously read the process-smoke fingerprint from the live repository, which the parallel `dotnet test -c Release` lane rewrites underneath it, so the test failed whenever `publish/` was not produced by the immediately preceding build. It now resolves the fingerprint from a fixture artifact pair it controls.
- The published Worker is now byte-identical to the Worker that the test lanes and the release process-smoke lane actually execute. The solution maps `Release|Any CPU` to the Worker's `x86` project platform while `build.ps1` compiled the project directly as `AnyCPU`, so `publish/` carried different bytes; the preflight gate then failed closed with "Process smoke binaries could not be bound to the current publish artifacts", and a full preflight could not pass. `build.ps1` now builds the Worker with the solution's platform, guarded by a test that keeps the two mappings in sync.
- `build.ps1` now emits the Gateway's non-RID Release build alongside the publish. `dotnet publish -o <dir>` never writes `bin/Release/net10.0-windows`, and the SDK stamps the commit into the assembly, so the copy the release certificate compares byte-for-byte went stale after any commit and silently invalidated the process-smoke fingerprint.
- Lifecycle build/specify work now uses a per-Worker FIFO with normalized coalescing, queue metadata, terminal waiting, warning deltas, unified Gateway/Worker operation aliases, and registry-backed long actions (`validate-kb`, `reorg`, and forced `index`). Status shaping also keeps the reserved `gateway:metrics` target out of operation-id validation. ([#305](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/305))
- Source-store population now resumes from a persisted cursor, refreshes stale parts in bounded low-priority slices, reports backfill state in index diagnostics, and lifts the legacy fixed full-source budget only after certified coverage. ([#307](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/307))
- Report layout control mutations now support typed add/move/remove operations with geometry and font metadata, relative placement, overlap checks, optimistic versions, dry-run diffs, independent read-back, and rollback. ([#314](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/314))
- Runtime garbage collection now retains staged directories used by live Gateway/broker/Worker processes, fails closed when process inspection is incomplete, rechecks before and after an atomic rename, retries tombstones safely, and reports retained PIDs in `doctor` instead of partially deleting an active runtime. ([#304](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/304))
- Post-timeout recovery fences now use the tool's real affected part, accept complete versioned whole-object reads, expand multi-part reads, and provide an exact `limit: 0` recovery hint. ([#311](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/311))
- Source search store eligibility and hits are now part-aware: explicit `webForm`, `rules`, `conditions`, and `layout` scopes are not discarded because another part is cached, and stored hits retain their actual part label. ([#312](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/312))
- WorkWithPlus grid-column and presentation-variable actions now require stable SDK identity, verify the parent/grid projection and variable declaration after save, and refuse ambiguous or unverifiable mutations. ([#268](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/268))
- Source search continuation, shared-host routing, and exact cached-read identity now remain scoped to the originating worker/KB and preserve bounded, fail-closed part coverage. ([#306](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/306))
- Legacy WebForm edits preserve untouched `CaptionExpression`/event semantics, preserve sibling shape, and support explicit identity-safe, read-back-validated add/visibility/remove/fieldset operations. ([#308](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/308), [#309](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/309), [#310](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/310))
- K2B WebPanel designer metadata is read-only and protected ranges cannot be edited, while structural variable parts remain readable through legacy/proxy shapes. Grid edits are now classified offline against the aspects K2BTools actually materializes into its generated `Do Not Change` methods: display-only edits (column title/caption/order) are proven independent and proceed on the normal verified write path, while base table, key attributes, selection mode, conditions, ordering and column-set changes are refused with an explicit regeneration-impact report instead of a blanket refusal. The classification derives its dependency facts from the real Events source and fails closed on anything it cannot prove. ([#313](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/313), [#315](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/315), [#316](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/316))
- Soft reload now shares the Gateway/Worker durable jobs path and merges snapshots without resurrecting terminal jobs or cancelling still-live admission waiters. ([#305](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/305))
- Recovery journals now create only exact affected-part fences, preserve homonymous identities independently, cover batch I/O mutations, recognize empty enumerated parts in complete whole-object reads, and reconcile late persisted results without clearing canceled-operation protection. ([#311](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/311))
- The live harness now records diagnostic error counts and turns actual Worker/Gateway errors, missing diagnostic evidence, explicit-KB open failures, failed cleanup, or missing process-exit evidence into `unavailable` instead of reporting a false pass. ([#305](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/305), [#306](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/306))
- WebForm independent read-back failures now default to guarded rollback when a post-save version is available, and report an explicit deferred recovery state when a safe compensating write cannot be proven. ([#308](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/308), [#309](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/309), [#310](https://github.com/lennix1337/Genexus18MCP/issues/310))
